\displaystyle h^{2\beta}=n_{0}^{-1}\wedge(n/\gamma)^{-\frac{2\beta}{2\beta+1}}=\left\{n_{0}\vee(n/\gamma)^{\frac{2\beta}{2\beta+1}}\right\}^{-1}\,.

Now we can plug the values of
n_{0}\vee\frac{h}{\gamma}\sum_{k=1}^{K}n_{k}
and
h^{2\beta}
into
{\sf KL}({\mathbb{P}}_{0}\ \|\ {\mathbb{P}}_{1})
and get

\begin{split}{\sf KL}({\mathbb{P}}_{0}\ \|\ {\mathbb{P}}_{1})&\leq(2\vee 4a_{0})L^{2}c_{0}^{2}h^{2\beta}\left(n_{0}\vee\frac{h}{\gamma}\sum_{k=1}^{K}n_{k}\right)\\
&\leq(2\vee 4a_{0})L^{2}c_{0}^{2}\left\{n_{0}\vee(n/\gamma)^{\frac{2\beta}{2\beta+1}}\right\}^{-1}\left\{n_{0}\vee\left(n/\gamma\right)^{\frac{2\beta}{2\beta+1}}\right\}\\
&=(2\vee 4a_{0})L^{2}c_{0}^{2}\,.\end{split}

Again, using the lower bound in Equation (
B.8
) we have

\begin{split}\inf_{\hat{{\bm{\theta}}}(u_{0})}\sup_{\begin{subarray}{c}\forall j\in[p],\\
\theta_{j}\in\mathcal{H}(\beta,L)\end{subarray}}\mathbb{E}\left(\|\hat{\bm{\theta}}(u_{0})-{\bm{\theta}}(u_{0})\|_{A}^{2}\right)&\geq\frac{r_{n,K,\gamma}^{2}}{2}\left(1-\sqrt{(1\vee 2a_{0})L^{2}c_{0}^{2}}\right)\\
&=\frac{\lambda_{A,1}L^{2}c_{0}^{2}\left(n_{0}^{-1}\wedge(n/\gamma)^{-\frac{2\beta}{2\beta+1}}\right)}{18e^{2}}\left(1-\sqrt{(1\vee 2a_{0})L^{2}c_{0}^{2}}\right)\end{split}

(B.15)

Same as Case 1, we can set
c_{0}=C_{0}\wedge\frac{1}{2\sqrt{(1\vee 2a_{0})L^{2}}}
to find lower bound

\geq\frac{\lambda_{A,1}L^{2}c_{0}^{2}}{36e^{2}}\left(n_{0}^{-1}\wedge(n/\gamma)^{-\frac{2\beta}{2\beta+1}}\right)\,.

Summarizing Case 1 - Case 3:

By Equation (
B.12
), we find the maximum of the lower bounds on in Equations (
B.13
) - (
B.15
), it follows that

\begin{split}\inf_{\hat{{\bm{\theta}}}(u_{0})}\sup_{\begin{subarray}{c}\forall j\in[p],\\
\theta_{j}\in\mathcal{H}(\beta,L)\end{subarray}}\mathbb{E}\left(\|\hat{\bm{\theta}}(u_{0})-{\bm{\theta}}(u_{0})\|_{A}^{2}\right)\geq C\cdot\frac{1}{n_{0}}\wedge\max\left\{\left(\frac{K}{\gamma}\right)^{-2\beta},\left(\frac{n}{\gamma}\right)^{-\frac{2\beta}{2\beta+1}},\frac{1}{n}\right\}\,,\end{split}

where

C=\frac{\lambda_{A,1}L^{2}c_{0}^{2}}{36e^{2}}\qquad\text{for }c_{0}=C_{0}\wedge\frac{1}{2\sqrt{(1\vee 2a_{0})L^{2}}}.

B.5

Proof of Theorem
3.10

Proof B.5

Linear regression is a special GLM with squared loss
\ell(\eta,y)=\frac{1}{2}(y-\eta)^{2}
.
Accordingly we focus on the major changes relative to proof of Lemma
A.10
. Readers may refer to part 2 of Appendix
C.9
for the proof under a more general setup. The norm
\|\cdot\|
used in the proof denotes the
\ell_{2}
-norm when applied to a vector, and the spectral norm when applied to a matrix.

Write
\Psi(u_{0})=\mathbb{E}[XX^{\top}\mid U=u_{0}]
.
Let
{\bm{\tau}}_{\mathrm{TL}}\coloneqq\hat{\bm{\theta}}_{\mathrm{TL}}(u_{0})-{\bm{\theta}}(u_{0})
,

{\bm{\tau}}_{\mathrm{DVCM}}\coloneqq\hat{\bm{\theta}}_{\mathrm{DVCM}}(u_{0})-{\bm{\theta}}(u_{0})
,
and
{\bm{\tau}}_{\mathrm{LR}}\coloneqq\hat{\bm{\theta}}_{\mathrm{LR}}(u_{0})-{\bm{\theta}}(u_{0})
. Recall the rates of
\hat{\bm{\theta}}_{\mathrm{LR}}(u_{0})
,
\hat{\bm{\theta}}_{\mathrm{DVCM}}(u_{0})
, and their ratio

{\bm{\tau}}_{\mathrm{LR}}=O_{p}(r_{\mathrm{LR}}),\quad{\bm{\tau}}_{\mathrm{DVCM}}=O_{p}(r_{\mathrm{DVCM}}),\qquad\rho\coloneqq\frac{r_{\mathrm{LR}}}{r_{\mathrm{DVCM}}},

and by construction
Q=\hat{Q}\asymp_{p}\rho^{2}I
, i.e.
c\rho^{2}\leq\lambda_{\min}(Q)\leq\lambda_{\max}(Q)\leq C\rho^{2}
w.p.
\to 1
.

Consider the TL objective with linear DVCM:

L_{n}(\alpha)=\frac{1}{2n_{0}}\sum_{i\in\mathcal{I}_{0}^{*}}\big(Y_{0i}-X_{0i}^{\top}\alpha\big)^{2}+\frac{1}{2}\|\alpha-\hat{\bm{\theta}}_{\mathrm{DVCM}}(u_{0})\|_{Q}^{2}.

Taking the gradient and setting it to zero at
\alpha=\hat{\bm{\theta}}_{\mathrm{TL}}(u_{0})
gives

\frac{1}{n_{0}}\mathbf{X}_{0}^{\top}\big(\mathbf{X}_{0}\hat{\bm{\theta}}_{\mathrm{TL}}(u_{0})-\mathbf{Y}_{0}\big)+Q\big(\hat{\bm{\theta}}_{\mathrm{TL}}(u_{0})-\hat{\bm{\theta}}_{\mathrm{DVCM}}(u_{0})\big)=0,

where
\mathbf{X}_{0}\in{\mathbb{R}}^{n_{0}\times p}
with
X_{0i}
being its
i^{th}
row,
\mathbf{Y}_{0}\in{\mathbb{R}}^{n_{0}}
with entries being
Y_{0i}
. Let
{\bm{\epsilon}}_{0}\in{\mathbb{R}}^{n_{0}}
be a vector with
\varepsilon_{0i}
being its
i^{th}
entry. Substitute

\mathbf{Y}_{0}=\mathbf{X}_{0}{\bm{\theta}}(u_{0})+{\bm{\epsilon}}_{0}
and
\hat{\bm{\theta}}_{\mathrm{TL}}(u_{0})={\bm{\theta}}(u_{0})+{\bm{\tau}}_{\mathrm{TL}}
:

\frac{1}{n_{0}}\mathbf{X}_{0}^{\top}\big(\mathbf{X}_{0}{\bm{\tau}}_{\mathrm{TL}}-{\bm{\epsilon}}_{0}\big)+Q\big({\bm{\tau}}_{\mathrm{TL}}-{\bm{\tau}}_{\mathrm{DVCM}}\big)=0\ \ \Longleftrightarrow\ \ {\bm{\tau}}_{\mathrm{TL}}=\Big(\tfrac{1}{n_{0}}\mathbf{X}_{0}^{\top}\mathbf{X}_{0}+Q\Big)^{-1}\left(Q\,{\bm{\tau}}_{\mathrm{DVCM}}+\tfrac{1}{n_{0}}\mathbf{X}_{0}^{\top}{\bm{\epsilon}}_{0}\right).

Moreover, the involved components have the following limits:

\displaystyle\tfrac{1}{n_{0}}\mathbf{X}_{0}^{\top}\mathbf{X}_{0}=\Psi(u_{0})+o_{p}(1),\quad

\displaystyle\tfrac{1}{n_{0}}\mathbf{X}_{0}^{\top}{\bm{\epsilon}}_{0}\ =\ \Big(\tfrac{1}{n_{0}}\mathbf{X}_{0}^{\top}\mathbf{X}_{0}\Big){\bm{\tau}}_{\mathrm{LR}}\ \implies\ \tfrac{1}{n_{0}}\mathbf{X}_{0}^{\top}{\bm{\epsilon}}_{0}=(\Psi(u_{0})+o_{p}(1))\,{\bm{\tau}}_{\mathrm{LR}},\qquad(\because\text{OLS solution}).

so the linear representation is written as

\begin{split}{\bm{\tau}}_{\mathrm{TL}}&=\big(\Psi(u_{0})+o_{p}(1)+Q\big)^{-1}\Big\{\,Q\,{\bm{\tau}}_{\mathrm{DVCM}}+(\Psi(u_{0})+o_{p}(1)){\bm{\tau}}_{\mathrm{LR}}\Big\}\\
&=\big(\Psi(u_{0})+Q\big)^{-1}\Big\{\,Q\,{\bm{\tau}}_{\mathrm{DVCM}}+\Psi(u_{0}){\bm{\tau}}_{\mathrm{LR}}\Big\}(1+o_{p}(1)).\end{split}

(B.16)

By sample splitting,
{\bm{\tau}}_{\mathrm{LR}}
is independent of
{\bm{\tau}}_{\mathrm{DVCM}}
. Now we discuss the two cases
\rho\to 0
and
\rho\to\infty
.

Regime
\rho\to 0
(LR–dominated).

The properties
\rho\coloneqq r_{\mathrm{LR}}/r_{\mathrm{DVCM}}\to 0
and
Q=O_{p}(\rho^{2})
jointly imply the denominator in (
B.16
) is

\big(\Psi(u_{0})+Q\big)^{-1}=\Psi(u_{0})^{-1}+o_{p}(1)
. Moreover,

\|\Psi^{-1}(u_{0})Q{\bm{\tau}}_{\mathrm{DVCM}}\|=O_{p}(\rho^{2}r_{\mathrm{DVCM}})=o_{p}(r_{\mathrm{LR}})
.
Thus, from the representation in (
B.16
),

{\bm{\tau}}_{\mathrm{TL}}={\bm{\tau}}_{\mathrm{LR}}+o_{p}(r_{\mathrm{LR}}).

By Slutsky’s theorem and part 1 of Proposition
A.8
, it follows that

r_{\mathrm{LR}}^{-1}\big(\hat{\bm{\theta}}_{\mathrm{TL}}(u_{0})-{\bm{\theta}}(u_{0})\big)\xrightarrow{d}\mathcal{N}\big(0,\ \Omega_{\mathrm{LR}}(u_{0})\big).

Regime
\rho\to\infty
(DVCM–dominated).

Start from the linearization

{\bm{\tau}}_{\mathrm{TL}}=(\Psi(u_{0})+Q)^{-1}\left\{\,Q\,{\bm{\tau}}_{\mathrm{DVCM}}+\Psi(u_{0})\,{\bm{\tau}}_{\mathrm{LR}}\right\}+o_{p}(r_{\mathrm{TL}})(1+o_{p}(1)).

Use the identity

(\Psi(u_{0})+Q)^{-1}Q=(\Psi(u_{0})+Q)^{-1}(\Psi(u_{0})+Q-\Psi(u_{0}))=I-(\Psi(u_{0})+Q)^{-1}\Psi(u_{0}),

to rewrite

{\bm{\tau}}_{\mathrm{TL}}=\left\{{\bm{\tau}}_{\mathrm{DVCM}}-(\Psi(u_{0})+Q)^{-1}\Psi(u_{0})\,{\bm{\tau}}_{\mathrm{DVCM}}+(\Psi(u_{0})+Q)^{-1}\Psi(u_{0})\,{\bm{\tau}}_{\mathrm{LR}}\right\}(1+o_{p}(1)).

(B.17)

Since
\lambda_{\min}(Q)\asymp_{p}\rho^{2}
and
\Psi(u_{0})\succeq c_{0}^{\prime}I
, we have

\|(\Psi(u_{0})+Q)^{-1}\|\leq\|Q^{-1}\|=O_{p}(\rho^{-2}),\qquad\|\Psi\|=O(1).

Hence it follows that

\|(\Psi(u_{0})+Q)^{-1}\Psi(u_{0})\,{\bm{\tau}}_{\mathrm{DVCM}}\|\leq\|(\Psi(u_{0})+Q)^{-1}\|\,\|\Psi(u_{0})\|\,\|{\bm{\tau}}_{\mathrm{DVCM}}\|=O_{p}(\rho^{-2}r_{\mathrm{DVCM}})=o_{p}(r_{\mathrm{DVCM}}),

and, using
r_{\mathrm{LR}}=\rho\,r_{\mathrm{DVCM}}
,

\displaystyle\|(\Psi(u_{0})+Q)^{-1}\Psi(u_{0}){\bm{\tau}}_{\mathrm{LR}}\|\leq\|(\Psi(u_{0})+Q)^{-1}\|\,\|\Psi(u_{0})\|\|{\bm{\tau}}_{\mathrm{LR}}\|

\displaystyle=O_{p}(\rho^{-2}r_{\mathrm{LR}})=O_{p}(r_{\mathrm{DVCM}}/\rho)=o_{p}(r_{\mathrm{DVCM}}).

Hence, going back to Equation (
B.17
), this yields

{\bm{\tau}}_{\mathrm{TL}}={\bm{\tau}}_{\mathrm{DVCM}}+o_{p}(r_{\mathrm{DVCM}}).

Therefore, by Slutsky’s theorem and part 2 of Proposition
A.8

r_{\mathrm{DVCM}}^{-1}\Big(\hat{\bm{\theta}}_{\mathrm{TL}}(u_{0})-{\bm{\theta}}(u_{0})-\mathbf{b}_{\mathrm{DVCM}}(u_{0})\Big)\ \xrightarrow{d}\ \mathcal{N}\big(0,\ \Omega_{\mathrm{DVCM}}(u_{0})\big).

Proposition
A.8
also implies that

r_{\mathrm{DVCM}}^{2}\asymp h^{2\beta}+\gamma/nh,\qquad\mathbf{b}_{\mathrm{DVCM}}(u_{0})\lesssim h^{\beta}

and thus the condition
nh^{2\beta+1}/\gamma\to 0
implies
r_{\mathrm{DVCM}}^{-1}\mathbf{b}_{\mathrm{DVCM}}(u_{0})=o_{p}(1)
, and this proves the theorem.

B.6

Proof of Theorem
3.13

Proof B.6

It is shown in Proposition
A.9
that

\big\|\hat{\bm{\theta}}_{\mathrm{GDVCM}}(u_{0})-{\bm{\theta}}(u_{0})\big\|_{2}^{2}=O_{p}\big(h^{2\beta}\big)+O_{p}\Big(\frac{\gamma}{nh}\Big).

and
h
is set to be

{\sf med}\left(e_{0}(n/\gamma)^{-\frac{1}{2\beta+1}},d_{(1)}(u_{0}),d_{(K)}(u_{0})\right)={\sf med}\left(O\left((n/\gamma)^{-\frac{1}{2\beta+1}}\right),O_{p}(\gamma/K),O_{p}(\gamma)\right),

where the order of
d_{(1)}(u_{0})
and
d_{(K)}(u_{0})
are discussed in Lemma
A.1
.
Then it follows that the bias-squared and variance of DVCM are of order

\displaystyle O_{p}\left(h^{2\beta}\right)={\sf med}\left(O_{p}\left((n/\gamma)^{-\frac{2\beta}{2\beta+1}}\right),O_{p}\left((\gamma/K)^{2\beta}\right),O_{p}\left(\gamma^{2\beta}\right)\right)\,,

\displaystyle O_{p}\left(\gamma/nh\right)={\sf med}\left(O_{p}\left((n/\gamma)^{-\frac{2\beta}{2\beta+1}}\right),O_{p}\left(K/n\right),O_{p}\left(n^{-1}\right)\right)\,.

We discuss the rates of
O_{p}\left(h^{2\beta}\right)
and
O_{p}\left(\gamma/nh\right)
under three circumstances:

i)\ n\gamma^{2\beta}\gg K^{2\beta+1},\qquad ii)\ n\gamma^{2\beta}\ll 1,\qquad iii)\ 1\lesssim n\gamma^{2\beta}\lesssim K^{2\beta+1}\,.

Case 1:
At first we consider the case
n\gamma^{2\beta}\gg K^{2\beta+1}
. This implies:

n\gamma^{2\beta}\gg K^{2\beta+1}\implies(n/\gamma)^{-2\beta/(2\beta+1)}\ll(\gamma/K)^{2\beta}\,.

Also,
K\geq 1
immediately imply
(\gamma/K)^{2\beta}\lesssim\gamma^{2\beta}
. Thus, it follows that

O_{p}\left(h^{2\beta}\right)=O_{p}\left((\gamma/K)^{2\beta}\right)\,

by its definition. Moreover, the same condition
n\gamma^{2\beta}\gg K^{2\beta+1}
also implies that

n\gamma^{2\beta}\gg K^{2\beta+1}\implies K/n\ll(\gamma/K)^{2\beta}

where the
K/n
term has lower bound

n^{-1}\lesssim K/n\,.

Thus, we have shown that

(\gamma/K)^{2\beta}\gtrsim K/n\vee n^{-1}\vee(n/\gamma)^{-2\beta/(2\beta+1)},

and this means

O_{p}\left(\gamma/nh\right)\lesssim O_{p}\left(h^{2\beta}\right)=O_{p}\left((\gamma/K)^{2\beta}\right),

so we conclude in this case

\left\|\hat{\bm{\theta}}_{\mathrm{GDVCM}}(u_{0})-{\bm{\theta}}(u_{0})\right\|_{2}^{2}=O_{p}\left((\gamma/K)^{2\beta}\right)\,.

Case 2:
In this case, we assume that
n\gamma^{2\beta}\ll 1\lesssim K^{2\beta+1}
. The first part of inequality implies that

n\gamma^{2\beta}\ll 1\implies(n/\gamma)^{-\frac{2\beta}{2\beta+1}}\ll n^{-1}

while
K\geq 1
immediately yields

K/n\gtrsim n^{-1}\,.

Therefore, by its definition,

O_{p}\left(\gamma/nh\right)=O_{p}\left(n^{-1}\right)\,.

The condition
n\gamma^{2\beta}\ll 1
also implies that

\displaystyle n\gamma^{2\beta}\ll 1\implies n\gamma^{2\beta}\ll K^{2\beta}\implies(\gamma/K)^{2\beta}\ll n^{-1}\,,

\displaystyle n\gamma^{2\beta}\ll 1\implies\gamma^{2\beta}\ll n^{-1}\,.

Hence, we have shown that

O_{p}\left(\gamma/nh\right)=O_{p}\left(n^{-1}\right)\gg O_{p}\left(h^{2\beta}\right)={\sf med}\left(O_{p}\left((n/\gamma)^{-\frac{2\beta}{2\beta+1}}\right),O_{p}\left((\gamma/K)^{2\beta}\right),O_{p}\left(\gamma^{2\beta}\right)\right)\,.

and therefore

\left\|\hat{\bm{\theta}}_{\mathrm{GDVCM}}(u_{0})-{\bm{\theta}}(u_{0})\right\|_{2}^{2}=O_{p}\left(n^{-1}\right)\,.

Case 3:
In this case, we consider the last case,
1\lesssim n\gamma^{2\beta}\lesssim K^{2\beta+1}
. This condition implies

\displaystyle n\gamma^{2\beta}\lesssim K^{2\beta+1}\implies(n/\gamma)^{-\frac{2\beta}{2\beta+1}}\gtrsim(\gamma/K)^{2\beta}\,,

\displaystyle 1\lesssim n\gamma^{2\beta}\implies\gamma^{2\beta}\gtrsim(n/\gamma)^{-\frac{2\beta}{2\beta+1}}\,.

Thus by definition

O_{p}\left(h^{2\beta}\right)=O_{p}\left((n/\gamma)^{-\frac{2\beta}{2\beta+1}}\right)\,.

Moreover, the same condition also implies

\displaystyle n\gamma^{2\beta}\lesssim K^{2\beta+1}\implies(n/\gamma)^{-\frac{2\beta}{2\beta+1}}\lesssim K/n\,,

\displaystyle 1\lesssim n\gamma^{2\beta}\implies n^{-1}\lesssim(n/\gamma)^{-\frac{2\beta}{2\beta+1}}\,.

Thus by definition

O_{p}\left(\gamma/nh\right)=O_{p}\left((n/\gamma)^{-\frac{2\beta}{2\beta+1}}\right)\,.

Therefore, in this case

\left\|\hat{\bm{\theta}}_{\mathrm{GDVCM}}(u_{0})-{\bm{\theta}}(u_{0})\right\|_{2}^{2}=O_{p}\left((n/\gamma)^{-\frac{2\beta}{2\beta+1}}\right)\,.

Collecting Case 1 to Case 3.

Collecting the the results from
Case 1
to
Case 3
, we have established that

\begin{split}&\left\|\hat{\bm{\theta}}_{\mathrm{GDVCM}}(u_{0})-{\bm{\theta}}(u_{0})\right\|_{2}^{2}=\begin{cases}O_{p}\left((K/\gamma)^{-2\beta}\right)&\text{if}\quad n\gamma^{2\beta}\gg K^{2\beta+1}\gtrsim 1,\\
O_{p}\left((n/\gamma)^{-\frac{2\beta}{2\beta+1}}\right)&\text{if}\quad 1\lesssim n\gamma^{2\beta}\lesssim K^{2\beta+1},\\
O_{p}\left(n^{-1}\right)&\text{if}\quad n\gamma^{2\beta}\ll 1\lesssim K^{2\beta+1}\,.\end{cases}\end{split}

Now we can apply the same argument in the last part of Appendix
B.1
(“Justification of maximal upper bounds”) to conclude that

\left\|\hat{\bm{\theta}}_{\mathrm{GDVCM}}(u_{0})-{\bm{\theta}}(u_{0})\right\|_{2}^{2}=O_{p}\left(\max\left\{(K/\gamma)^{-2\beta},(n/\gamma)^{-\frac{2\beta}{2\beta+1}},n^{-1}\right\}\right)\,.

Now we have established the rate of
\hat{\bm{\theta}}_{\mathrm{GDVCM}}(u_{0})
and we will move forward to
\hat{\bm{\theta}}_{\mathrm{TL}}(u_{0})
. It has been shown in Lemma
A.10
that

\displaystyle\left\|\hat{\bm{\theta}}_{\mathrm{TL}}(u_{0})-{\bm{\theta}}(u_{0})\right\|_{2}^{2}=O_{p}\left(r_{\mathrm{GLR}}^{2}\wedge r_{\mathrm{GDVCM}}^{2}\right)\,.

The rate
r_{\mathrm{GLR}}^{2}=n_{0}^{-1}
so we conclude that

\displaystyle\left\|\hat{\bm{\theta}}_{\mathrm{TL}}(u_{0})-{\bm{\theta}}(u_{0})\right\|_{2}^{2}=O_{p}\left(n_{0}^{-1}\wedge\max\left\{(K/\gamma)^{-2\beta},(n/\gamma)^{-\frac{2\beta}{2\beta+1}},n^{-1}\right\}\right)\,.

B.7

Proof of Theorem
3.14

Proof B.7

Let
{\bm{\tau}}_{\mathrm{TL}}\coloneqq\hat{\bm{\theta}}_{\mathrm{TL}}(u_{0})-{\bm{\theta}}(u_{0})
,

{\bm{\tau}}_{\mathrm{GDVCM}}\coloneqq\hat{\bm{\theta}}_{\mathrm{GDVCM}}(u_{0})-{\bm{\theta}}(u_{0})
,
and
{\bm{\tau}}_{\mathrm{GLR}}\coloneqq\hat{\bm{\theta}}_{\mathrm{GLR}}(u_{0})-{\bm{\theta}}(u_{0})
.
Recall the expansion in Equation (
B.16
)

{\bm{\tau}}_{\mathrm{TL}}=\big(\Psi(u_{0})+\hat{Q}\big)^{-1}\Big\{\hat{Q}\,{\bm{\tau}}_{\mathrm{GDVCM}}+\Psi(u_{0})\,{\bm{\tau}}_{\mathrm{GLR}}\Big\}\,\{1+o_{p}(1)\},

(B.18)

and set
B_{Q}\coloneqq\Psi(u_{0})+\hat{Q}
. It is shown in Proposition
A.9
that the asymptotic bias and variance for DVCM are of orders
O(h^{\beta})
and
O(\gamma/(nh))
. Therefore the condition
nh^{2\beta+1}/\gamma\to 0
leads to the asymptotic unbiasedness of DVCM

\sqrt{\frac{nh}{\gamma}}\left(\hat{\bm{\theta}}_{\mathrm{GDVCM}}(u_{0})-{\bm{\theta}}(u_{0})\right)\xrightarrow{d}\mathcal{N}\left(0,\Omega_{\mathrm{GDVCM}}(u_{0})\right)\,.

Therefore, by Proposition
A.9
, and with definition on the variance for
\mathrm{GLR}
and
\mathrm{GDVCM}
,

V_{\mathrm{GLR}}(u_{0}):=\frac{1}{n_{0}}\Omega_{\mathrm{GLR}}(u_{0}),\quad V_{\mathrm{GDVCM}}(u_{0}):=\frac{\gamma}{nh}\Omega_{\mathrm{GDVCM}}(u_{0})

it follows that

V_{\mathrm{GLR}}^{-1/2}(u_{0})\,{\bm{\tau}}_{\mathrm{GLR}}\ \xrightarrow{d}\ \mathcal{N}\big(0,I\big),\qquad V_{\mathrm{GDVCM}}^{-1/2}(u_{0})\,{\bm{\tau}}_{\mathrm{GDVCM}}\ \xrightarrow{d}\ \mathcal{N}\big(0,I\big).

Under sample splitting, the two terms
{\bm{\tau}}_{\mathrm{GLR}},{\bm{\tau}}_{\mathrm{GDVCM}}
are independent of each other. Since
\Psi(u_{0})
is positive definite
and
\hat{Q}
is positive semidefinite,
B_{Q}
is invertible w.p.
\to 1
.
By the continuous mapping theorem,

\Sigma_{1}^{-1/2}\left[B_{Q}^{-1}\Psi(u_{0})\,{\bm{\tau}}_{\mathrm{GLR}}\right]\ \xrightarrow{d}\ \mathcal{N}\Big(0,I\Big),\quad\Sigma_{1}=B_{Q}^{-1}\Psi(u_{0})\,V_{\mathrm{GLR}}(u_{0})\,\Psi(u_{0})\,B_{Q}^{-1}

and

\Sigma_{2}^{-1/2}\left[B_{Q}^{-1}\hat{Q}\,{\bm{\tau}}_{\mathrm{GDVCM}}\right]\ \xrightarrow{d}\ \mathcal{N}\Big(0,I\Big),\quad\Sigma_{2}=B_{Q}^{-1}\hat{Q}\,V_{\mathrm{GDVCM}}(u_{0})\,\hat{Q}\,B_{Q}^{-1}

Independence implies their covariances are additive. Using
(
B.18
) and by Slutsky’s theorem,

\Sigma_{\mathrm{TL}}^{-1/2}\,{\bm{\tau}}_{\mathrm{TL}}\ \xrightarrow{d}\ \mathcal{N}(0,I),

where

\displaystyle\Sigma_{\mathrm{TL}}

\displaystyle=\Sigma_{1}+\Sigma_{2}

\displaystyle=B_{Q}^{-1}\Psi(u_{0})\,V_{\mathrm{GLR}}(u_{0})\,\Psi(u_{0})\,B_{Q}^{-1}+B_{Q}^{-1}\hat{Q}\,V_{\mathrm{GDVCM}}(u_{0})\,\hat{Q}\,B_{Q}^{-1}\,.

For feasible inference, let
\hat{\Psi}(u_{0})
,
\hat{V}_{\mathrm{GLR}}(u_{0})
, and
\hat{V}_{\mathrm{GDVCM}}(u_{0})
be consistent estimators.
Define
\hat{\Sigma}_{\mathrm{TL}}
by replacing the population quantities in
\Sigma_{\mathrm{TL}}
with their estimators.
Then
\hat{\Sigma}_{\mathrm{TL}}=\Sigma_{\mathrm{TL}}\{1+o_{p}(1)\}
, and another application of Slutsky’s theorem yields

\hat{\Sigma}_{\mathrm{TL}}^{-1/2}\,{\bm{\tau}}_{\mathrm{TL}}\ \xrightarrow{d}\ \mathcal{N}(0,I).

B.8

Proof of Corollary
3.11

Proof B.8

This proof is the linear DVCM specialization of of the generalized DVCM derivation in Appendix
B.7
. it follows the same steps, but with
\Psi(u)=\mathbb{E}[XX^{\top}\mid U=u]
and the variance component
\sigma(\cdot)
specialized to the linear model.

Let
{\bm{\tau}}_{\mathrm{TL}}\coloneqq\hat{\bm{\theta}}_{\mathrm{TL}}(u_{0})-{\bm{\theta}}(u_{0})
,

{\bm{\tau}}_{\mathrm{DVCM}}\coloneqq\hat{\bm{\theta}}_{\mathrm{DVCM}}(u_{0})-{\bm{\theta}}(u_{0})
,
and
{\bm{\tau}}_{\mathrm{LR}}\coloneqq\hat{\bm{\theta}}_{\mathrm{LR}}(u_{0})-{\bm{\theta}}(u_{0})
.
In the linear case (quadratic loss), the same linearization as in (
B.16
) holds:

{\bm{\tau}}_{\mathrm{TL}}=\big(\Psi(u_{0})+\hat{Q}\big)^{-1}\Big\{\hat{Q}\,{\bm{\tau}}_{\mathrm{DVCM}}+\Psi(u_{0})\,{\bm{\tau}}_{\mathrm{LR}}\Big\}\,\{1+o_{p}(1)\},

(B.19)

and we write
B_{Q}\coloneqq\Psi(u_{0})+\hat{Q}
.
By Proposition
A.8
, the DVCM bias and variance are of orders

O(h^{\beta})
and
O(\gamma/(nh))
. Hence, if
nh^{2\beta+1}/\gamma\to 0
,
the bias is negligible and

\sqrt{\frac{nh}{\gamma}}\big(\hat{\bm{\theta}}_{\mathrm{DVCM}}(u_{0})-{\bm{\theta}}(u_{0})\big)\ \xrightarrow{d}\ \mathcal{N}\big(0,\ \Omega_{\mathrm{DVCM}}(u_{0})\big).

Moreover, by the same proposition, and with definition on the variance for
\mathrm{LR}
and
\mathrm{DVCM}
,

V_{\mathrm{LR}}(u_{0}):=\frac{1}{n_{0}}\Omega_{\mathrm{LR}}(u_{0}),\quad V_{\mathrm{DVCM}}(u_{0}):=\frac{\gamma}{nh}\Omega_{\mathrm{DVCM}}(u_{0})

it follows that

V_{\mathrm{LR}}^{-1/2}(u_{0})\,{\bm{\tau}}_{\mathrm{LR}}\ \xrightarrow{d}\ \mathcal{N}\big(0,I\big),\qquad V_{\mathrm{DVCM}}^{-1/2}(u_{0})\,{\bm{\tau}}_{\mathrm{DVCM}}\ \xrightarrow{d}\ \mathcal{N}\big(0,I\big).

and (by sample splitting) these limits are independent.
Since
\Psi(u_{0})\succ 0
and
\hat{Q}\succeq 0
,
B_{Q}
is invertible w.p.
\to 1
.
By the continuous mapping theorem,

\Sigma_{1}^{-1/2}\left[B_{Q}^{-1}\Psi(u_{0})\,{\bm{\tau}}_{\mathrm{LR}}\right]\ \xrightarrow{d}\ \mathcal{N}(0,I),\quad\Sigma_{1}:=B_{Q}^{-1}\Psi(u_{0})\,V_{\mathrm{LR}}(u_{0})\,\Psi(u_{0})\,B_{Q}^{-1},

and

\Sigma_{2}^{-1/2}\left[B_{Q}^{-1}\hat{Q}\,{\bm{\tau}}_{\mathrm{DVCM}}\right]\ \xrightarrow{d}\ \mathcal{N}(0,I),\quad\Sigma_{2}:=B_{Q}^{-1}\hat{Q}\,V_{\mathrm{DVCM}}(u_{0})\,\hat{Q}\,B_{Q}^{-1}.

Independence implies additivity of covariances. From (
B.19
),

{\bm{\tau}}_{\mathrm{TL}}=B_{Q}^{-1}\Psi(u_{0})\,{\bm{\tau}}_{\mathrm{LR}}+B_{Q}^{-1}\hat{Q}\,{\bm{\tau}}_{\mathrm{DVCM}}+o_{p}({\bm{\tau}}_{\mathrm{TL}}),

so by Slutsky’s theorem,

\Sigma_{\mathrm{TL}}^{-1/2}\,{\bm{\tau}}_{\mathrm{TL}}\ \xrightarrow{d}\ \mathcal{N}(0,I),\qquad\Sigma_{\mathrm{TL}}=\Sigma_{1}+\Sigma_{2}=B_{Q}^{-1}\Psi\,V_{\mathrm{LR}}\,\Psi B_{Q}^{-1}+B_{Q}^{-1}\hat{Q}\,V_{\mathrm{DVCM}}\,\hat{Q}B_{Q}^{-1},

For feasible inference, take consistent estimators

\hat{\Psi}(u_{0}),\ \hat{V}_{\mathrm{LR}}(u_{0}),\ \hat{V}_{\mathrm{DVCM}}(u_{0})
,
form
\hat{\Sigma}_{\mathrm{TL}}
by plug-in, and conclude

\hat{\Sigma}_{\mathrm{TL}}^{-1/2}\,{\bm{\tau}}_{\mathrm{TL}}\rightarrow_{d}\mathcal{N}(0,I)
.

Appendix C

Proof of auxiliary lemmas

C.1

Proof of part (1) of Lemma
A.1

Before going into the details, let’s lay down some notations. Assume that
f
is supported on
[a,b]
with
f\in[1/a_{0},a_{0}]
for some large
a_{0}>0
. We further assume without loss of generality that
(b-a)\leq a_{0}/2
. As the density of
U
is
f((u-u_{*})/\gamma)/\gamma
, it is immediate that:

F_{U}(u):={\mathbb{P}}(U\leq u)=F\left(\frac{u-u_{*}}{\gamma}\right)\,.

Hence
U
is supported on
[a\gamma+u_{*},b\gamma+u_{*}]
. From the definition of nearest neighbour, we have:

\displaystyle{\mathbb{P}}(d_{(1)}(u_{0})\geq\gamma t/K)={\mathbb{P}}\left(|U-u_{0}|\geq\gamma t/K\right)^{K}

\displaystyle=\left(1-{\mathbb{P}}(|U-u_{0}|\leq\gamma t/K)\right)^{K}

\displaystyle=\left[1-\left\{F\left(\frac{u_{0}-u_{*}}{\gamma}+\frac{t}{K}\right)-F\left(\frac{u_{0}-u_{*}}{\gamma}-\frac{t}{K}\right)\right\}\right]^{K}

Observe that the probability is not non-zero for all
t
. In fact
t
must have the following upper bound:

\gamma t/K\leq\max\left\{b\gamma+u_{*}-u_{0},\ u_{0}-u_{*}-a\gamma\right\}\coloneqq\gamma t_{0}\implies t\leq Kt_{0}\,.

Now let us concentrate on the upper bound. As
f\geq 1/a_{0}
, we have:

F\left(\frac{u_{0}-u_{*}}{\gamma}+\frac{t}{K}\right)-F\left(\frac{u_{0}-u_{*}}{\gamma}-\frac{t}{K}\right)\geq\frac{2t}{a_{0}K}\,,

and consequently:

1-\left\{F\left(\frac{u_{0}-u_{*}}{\gamma}+\frac{t}{K}\right)-F\left(\frac{u_{0}-u_{*}}{\gamma}-\frac{t}{K}\right)\right\}\leq 1-\frac{2t}{a_{0}K}\,.

This bound only makes sense only if
t\leq(a_{0}K)/2
. We now prove the lower bound on the probability, where we use the upper bound on
f
, i.e.,
f\leq a_{0}
. This implies:

1-\left\{F\left(\frac{u_{0}-u_{*}}{\gamma}+\frac{t}{K}\right)-F\left(\frac{u_{0}-u_{*}}{\gamma}-\frac{t}{K}\right)\right\}\geq 1-\frac{2a_{0}t}{K}\,.

As before, this bound only makes sense when
t\leq K/(2a_{0})
. Therefore, it is concluded that

\begin{split}\left\{1-\frac{2a_{0}t}{K}\right\}^{K}\mathbf{1}(t\leq K/(2a_{0}))\leq{\mathbb{P}}(d_{(1)}(u_{0})\geq\gamma t/K)\leq\left\{1-\frac{2t}{a_{0}K}\right\}^{K}\mathbf{1}(t\leq(a_{0}K)/2)\,.\end{split}

(C.1)

Now consider the expectation:

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left[(K/\gamma)^{\beta}d_{(1)}(u_{0})^{\beta}\right]

\displaystyle=\int_{0}^{\infty}{\mathbb{P}}\left((K/\gamma)^{\beta}d_{(1)}(u_{0})^{\beta}\geq t\right)\ dt

\displaystyle=\int_{0}^{\infty}{\mathbb{P}}\left(d_{(1)}(u_{0})\geq\frac{\gamma}{K}t^{\frac{1}{\beta}}\right)\ dt\,.

By the upper bound in Equation (
C.1
),

{\mathbb{P}}\left(d_{(1)}(u_{0})\geq\frac{\gamma}{K}t^{\frac{1}{\beta}}\right)\leq\left\{1-\frac{2t^{\frac{1}{\beta}}}{a_{0}K}\right\}^{K}\mathbf{1}(t^{\frac{1}{\beta}}\leq(a_{0}K)/2)\,

we obtain:

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left[(K/\gamma)^{\beta}d_{(1)}(u_{0})^{\beta}\right]

\displaystyle\leq\int_{0}^{\infty}\left(1-\frac{2t^{1/\beta}}{a_{0}K}\right)^{K}\ dt

\displaystyle\leq\int_{0}^{\infty}{\sf exp}\left(-\frac{2t^{1/\beta}}{a_{0}}\right)\ dt

\displaystyle\leq\int_{0}^{\infty}{\sf exp}\left(-\frac{2t^{1/\beta}}{a_{0}}\right)\ dt=C_{1}\,.

We now prove the lower bound on the expectation, where we use the lower bound in Equation (
C.1
),

{\mathbb{P}}\left(d_{(1)}(u_{0})\geq\frac{\gamma}{K}t^{\frac{1}{\beta}}\right)\geq\left\{1-\frac{2a_{0}t^{\frac{1}{\beta}}}{K}\right\}^{K}\mathbf{1}(t^{\frac{1}{\beta}}\leq K/(2a_{0}))\,

Define
t_{1}=\min\{t_{0},1/(2a_{0})\}
. We now have:

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left[(K/\gamma)^{\beta}d_{(1)}(u_{0})^{\beta}\right]

\displaystyle=\int_{0}^{(Kt_{0})^{\beta}}{\mathbb{P}}\left(d_{(1)}(u_{0})\geq\frac{\gamma}{K}t^{\frac{1}{\beta}}\right)\ dt

\displaystyle\geq\int_{0}^{(Kt_{1})^{\beta}}{\mathbb{P}}\left(d_{(1)}(u_{0})\geq\frac{\gamma}{K}t^{\frac{1}{\beta}}\right)\ dt

\displaystyle\geq\int_{0}^{(Kt_{1})^{\beta}}\left(1-\frac{2a_{0}t^{1/\beta}}{K}\right)^{K}\ dt

\displaystyle\geq\int_{0}^{(Kt_{1})^{\beta}}\ {\sf exp}\left(-2a_{0}t^{1/\beta}\right)\left(1-\frac{(2a_{0}t^{1/\beta})^{2}}{K}\right)\ dt\hskip 21.68121pt[\because(1+x/n)^{n}\geq e^{x}(1-x^{2}/n)]

\displaystyle\geq\int_{0}^{\frac{1}{C^{\beta}}K^{\beta/2}t_{0}^{\beta}}\ {\sf exp}\left(-2a_{0}t^{1/\beta}\right)\left(1-\frac{(2a_{0}t^{1/\beta})^{2}}{K}\right)\ dt\hskip 21.68121pt[\because K\geq 1,C>1\text{ to be chosen later}]

\displaystyle\geq\left(1-\left(\frac{2a_{0}t_{0}}{C}\right)^{2}\right)\int_{0}^{\frac{1}{C^{\beta}}K^{\beta/2}t_{1}^{\beta}}\ {\sf exp}\left(-2a_{0}t^{1/\beta}\right)\ dt

\displaystyle\geq\left(1-\left(\frac{2a_{0}t_{1}}{C}\right)^{2}\right)\int_{0}^{\frac{1}{C^{\beta}}t_{1}^{\beta}}\ {\sf exp}\left(-2a_{0}t^{1/\beta}\right)\ dt\hskip 21.68121pt[\because K\geq 1]

\displaystyle\geq C_{2}\,.

Now it is immediate that any
C>\max\{1,2a_{0}t_{0}\}
is a valid choice.

C.2

Proof of part (2) of Lemma
A.1

We use the same notation as before. Assume that
f
is supported on
[a,b]
with
f\in[1/a_{0},a_{0}]
for some large
a_{0}>0
. We further assume without loss of generality that
(b-a)\leq a_{0}/2
. As the density of
U
is
f((u-u_{*})/\gamma)/\gamma
, it is immediate that:

F_{U}(u)={\mathbb{P}}(U\leq u)=F\left(\frac{u-u_{*}}{\gamma}\right)\,.

Hence
U
is supported on
[a\gamma+u_{*},b\gamma+u_{*}]
. Recall that
d_{(K)}(u_{0})=\max_{k\in[K]}|U_{k}-u_{0}|
, it follows that

\displaystyle{\mathbb{P}}(d_{(K)}(u_{0})\geq\gamma t/K)=1-{\mathbb{P}}\left(|U-u_{0}|\leq\gamma t/K\right)^{K}=1-\left\{F\left(\frac{u_{0}-u_{*}}{\gamma}+\frac{t}{K}\right)-F\left(\frac{u_{0}-u_{*}}{\gamma}-\frac{t}{K}\right)\right\}^{K}

Observe that the probability is not non-zero for all
t
. In fact
t
must have the following upper bound:

\gamma t/K\leq\max\left\{b\gamma+u_{*}-u_{0},\ u_{0}-u_{*}-a\gamma\right\}\coloneqq\gamma t_{0}\implies t\leq Kt_{0}\,.

Now let us concentrate on the upper bound. As
f\geq 1/a_{0}
, we have:

F\left(\frac{u_{0}-u_{*}}{\gamma}+\frac{t}{K}\right)-F\left(\frac{u_{0}-u_{*}}{\gamma}-\frac{t}{K}\right)\geq\frac{2t}{a_{0}K}\,,

and consequently:

1-\left\{F\left(\frac{u_{0}-u_{*}}{\gamma}+\frac{t}{K}\right)-F\left(\frac{u_{0}-u_{*}}{\gamma}-\frac{t}{K}\right)\right\}\leq 1-\frac{2t}{a_{0}K}\,.

This bound only makes sense only if
t\leq(a_{0}K)/2
. We now prove the lower bound on the probability, where we use the upper bound on
f
, i.e.,
f\leq a_{0}
. This implies:

1-\left\{F\left(\frac{u_{0}-u_{*}}{\gamma}+\frac{t}{K}\right)-F\left(\frac{u_{0}-u_{*}}{\gamma}-\frac{t}{K}\right)\right\}\geq 1-\frac{2a_{0}t}{K}\,.

As before, this bound only makes sense when
t\leq K/(2a_{0})
. Therefore, it is concluded that

\begin{split}\left[1-\left\{\frac{2a_{0}t}{K}\right\}^{K}\right]\mathbf{1}(t\leq K/(2a_{0}))\leq{\mathbb{P}}(d_{(K)}(u_{0})\geq\gamma t/K)\leq\left[1-\left\{\frac{2t}{a_{0}K}\right\}^{K}\right]\mathbf{1}(t\leq(a_{0}K)/2)\,.\end{split}

(C.2)

Now consider the expectation
\mathbb{E}\left[d_{(K)}(u_{0})^{\beta}\right]
. Since
U
is supported on
[a\gamma+u_{*},b\gamma+u_{*}]
, whose Lebesgue measure
=(b-a)\gamma
, it is immediate that
\mathbb{E}\left[d_{(K)}(u_{0})^{\beta}\right]\leq(b-a)\gamma^{\beta}
.
Now consider the expectation:

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left[(K/\gamma)^{\beta}d_{(K)}(u_{0})^{\beta}\right]

\displaystyle=\int_{0}^{\infty}{\mathbb{P}}\left((K/\gamma)^{\beta}d_{(K)}(u_{0})^{\beta}\geq t\right)\ dt

\displaystyle=\int_{0}^{\infty}{\mathbb{P}}\left(d_{(K)}(u_{0})\geq\frac{\gamma}{K}t^{\frac{1}{\beta}}\right)\ dt\,.

By the lower bound in Equation (
C.2
),

{\mathbb{P}}\left(d_{(K)}(u_{0})\geq\frac{\gamma}{K}t^{\frac{1}{\beta}}\right)\geq\left[1-\left\{\frac{2a_{0}t^{\frac{1}{\beta}}}{K}\right\}^{K}\right]\mathbf{1}(t^{\frac{1}{\beta}}\leq K/(2a_{0}))\,.

Define
t_{1}=\min\{t_{0},1/(4a_{0})\}
. We now have:

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left[(K/\gamma)^{\beta}d_{(K)}(u_{0})^{\beta}\right]

\displaystyle=\int_{0}^{(Kt_{0})^{\beta}}{\mathbb{P}}\left(d_{(K)}(u_{0})\geq\frac{\gamma}{K}t^{\frac{1}{\beta}}\right)\ dt

\displaystyle\geq\int_{0}^{(Kt_{1})^{\beta}}{\mathbb{P}}\left(d_{(K)}(u_{0})\geq\frac{\gamma}{K}t^{\frac{1}{\beta}}\right)\ dt

\displaystyle\geq\int_{0}^{(Kt_{1})^{\beta}}1-\left(\frac{2a_{0}t^{1/\beta}}{K}\right)^{K}\ dt

\displaystyle=\int_{0}^{Kt_{1}}\left[1-\left(\frac{2a_{0}s}{K}\right)^{K}\right]\beta s^{\beta-1}\ ds\hskip 21.68121pt[\because\text{substitution }s=t^{\frac{1}{\beta}}]

\displaystyle\geq\left[1-\left(\frac{2a_{0}s}{K}\right)^{K}\right]\Bigg|_{s=K/(4a_{0})}\int_{0}^{Kt_{1}}\beta s^{\beta-1}\ ds

\displaystyle\geq\frac{1}{2}\int_{0}^{Kt_{1}}\beta s^{\beta-1}\ ds

\displaystyle=C_{2}K^{\beta}\,.

This means that
\mathbb{E}\left[d_{(K)}(u_{0})^{\beta}\right]\geq C_{2}\gamma^{\beta}
for some constant
C_{2}
.

C.3

Proof of Lemma
A.2

Let
\times
denote Cartesian product of two sets. Recall the definition of
Z_{ki}
:

Z_{ki}={\bm{\Phi}}_{l}\left(\frac{U_{k}-u_{0}}{h}\right)\otimes X_{ki}=\left\{X_{kij}\frac{1}{m!}\left(\frac{U_{k}-u_{0}}{h}\right)^{m}\right\}_{(j,m)\in\{1,\ldots,p\}\times\{0,1,\ldots,l\}}\in\mathbb{R}^{p(l+1)}\,.

Therefore,

\displaystyle\|Z_{ki}\|_{2}^{2}\mathbf{1}\{|U_{k}-u_{0}|\leq h\}

\displaystyle=\sum_{j=1}^{p}\sum_{m=0}^{l}X_{kij}^{2}\left(\frac{1}{m!}\left(\frac{U_{k}-u_{0}}{h}\right)^{m}\right)^{2}\mathbf{1}\{|U_{k}-u_{0}|\leq h\}

\displaystyle\leq\sum_{m=0}^{l}\left(\frac{1}{m!}\left(\frac{U_{k}-u_{0}}{h}\right)^{m}\right)^{2}\mathbf{1}\{|U_{k}-u_{0}|\leq h\}\hskip 21.68121pt[\because\|X_{ki}\|_{2}\leq 1]

\displaystyle\leq\sum_{m=0}^{l}\left(\frac{1}{m!}\right)^{2}

\displaystyle\leq\sum_{m=0}^{l}\left(\frac{1}{m!}\right)

\displaystyle\leq e\,.

Hence,

\|Z_{ki}\|_{2}^{2}\mathbf{1}\{|U_{k}-u_{0}|\leq h\}\leq e\implies\|Z_{ki}\|_{2}\mathbf{1}\{|U_{k}-u_{0}|\leq h\}\leq\sqrt{e}<2

C.4

Proof of Lemma
A.3

By Assumption
3.3
,
n_{k}\geq b_{0}^{\prime}\bar{n}
for all
k=1,\ldots,K
. Hence

S_{h}=\frac{1}{2}\sum_{k=0}^{K}n_{k}\mathbf{1}\{|U_{k}-u_{0}|\leq h\}\geq\frac{b_{0}^{\prime}\bar{n}}{2}\sum_{k=1}^{K}\mathbf{1}\{|U_{k}-u_{0}|\leq h\}.

Let
\mathcal{E}:=\{d_{(1)}(u_{0})\leq h\}
and pick an index
k_{0}
such that
|U_{k_{0}}-u_{0}|=d_{(1)}(u_{0})
. Then on
\mathcal{E}
,

S_{h}\geq\frac{b_{0}^{\prime}\bar{n}}{2}\Big(1+\sum_{k\in[K]\setminus\{k_{0}\}}\mathbf{1}\{|U_{k}-u_{0}|\leq h\}\Big)=\frac{b_{0}^{\prime}\bar{n}}{2}\,(S+1),

where
S:=\sum_{k\in[K]\setminus\{k_{0}\}}\mathbf{1}\{|U_{k}-u_{0}|\leq h\}
. Therefore,

\mathbb{E}\left[S_{h}^{-1}\mid\mathcal{E}\right]\leq\frac{2}{b_{0}^{\prime}\bar{n}}\,\mathbb{E}\left[\frac{1}{S+1}\,\middle|\,\mathcal{E}\right].

We now upper bound
\mathbb{E}[(S+1)^{-1}\mid\mathcal{E}]
, using the inequality

\mathbb{E}[(S+1)^{-1}\mid\mathcal{E}]\leq\frac{\mathbb{E}[(S+1)^{-1}]}{{\mathbb{P}}(\mathcal{E})}.

Unconditionally,
S\sim\mathrm{Bin}(K-1,p)
with

p={\mathbb{P}}(|U-u_{0}|\leq h)\ \geq\ \frac{a_{0}^{\prime}h}{\gamma}\qquad\text{[by Assumption~\ref{Assump:VCM-UX} (a) and condition }h\leq|\mathcal{U}|].

Since
S\sim\mathrm{Bin}(K-1,p)
,

\mathbb{E}\left[\frac{1}{S+1}\right]=\sum_{t=0}^{K-1}\frac{1}{t+1}\binom{K-1}{t}p^{t}(1-p)^{K-1-t}.

Use the identity

\frac{1}{t+1}\binom{K-1}{t}=\frac{1}{K}\binom{K}{t+1},

to obtain

\mathbb{E}\left[\frac{1}{S+1}\right]=\frac{1}{K}\sum_{t=0}^{K-1}\binom{K}{t+1}p^{t}(1-p)^{K-1-t}.

Reindex with
s=t+1
(so
s=1,\dots,K
) and factor one
p
:

\mathbb{E}\left[\frac{1}{S+1}\right]=\frac{1}{pK}\sum_{s=1}^{K}\binom{K}{s}p^{\,s}(1-p)^{K-s}=\frac{1}{pK}\Bigg(\sum_{s=0}^{K}\binom{K}{s}p^{\,s}(1-p)^{K-s}-(1-p)^{K}\Bigg).

Notice that the bracket equals
1-(1-p)^{K}
, hence

\mathbb{E}\left[\frac{1}{S+1}\right]=\frac{1-(1-p)^{K}}{pK}.

Moreover,
{\mathbb{P}}(\mathcal{E})=1-(1-p)^{K}
. Hence

\mathbb{E}\left[\frac{1}{S+1}\,\middle|\,\mathcal{E}\right]\leq\frac{\mathbb{E}[1/(S+1)]}{{\mathbb{P}}(\mathcal{E})}=\frac{1}{pK}\leq\frac{\gamma}{a_{0}^{\prime}Kh}.

Combining the bounds and using
n=(K+1)\bar{n}
,

\mathbb{E}\left[S_{h}^{-1}\mid\mathcal{E}\right]\leq\frac{2}{b_{0}^{\prime}\bar{n}}\cdot\frac{\gamma}{a_{0}^{\prime}Kh}\leq C\,\frac{\gamma}{nh},

for a constant
C>0
depending only on
a_{0}^{\prime},b_{0}^{\prime}
.

C.5

Proof of Lemma
A.4

Bias.
Recall

\hat{\bm{\theta}}_{\mathrm{DVCM}}(u_{0})=\mathbf{A}_{l}(\mathbf{Z}^{\top}\mathbf{W}\mathbf{Z})^{-1}\mathbf{Z}^{\top}\mathbf{W}\mathbf{y}.

Conditioning on
\Gamma:=\{(U_{k},X_{ki})\}
, write

\mathbf{y}=\mathbf{Z}{\bm{\alpha}}(u_{0})+\mathbf{r}+{\bm{\epsilon}},\qquad\mathbb{E}[{\bm{\epsilon}}\mid\Gamma]=0,

where
{\bm{\alpha}}(u_{0})\in\mathbb{R}^{(l+1)p}
collects the local polynomial coefficients at
u_{0}

(with
\mathbf{A}_{l}{\bm{\alpha}}(u_{0})={\bm{\theta}}(u_{0})
), and
\mathbf{r}\in\mathbb{R}^{n}
is the stacked approximation error
with entries
r_{ki}=X_{ki}^{\top}\{{\bm{\theta}}(U_{k})-\sum_{v=0}^{l}{\bm{\theta}}^{(v)}(u_{0})(U_{k}-u_{0})^{v}/v!\}
.
Then

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\!\left[\hat{\bm{\theta}}_{\mathrm{DVCM}}(u_{0})-{\bm{\theta}}(u_{0})\mid\Gamma\right]

\displaystyle=\mathbf{A}_{l}(\mathbf{Z}^{\top}\mathbf{W}\mathbf{Z})^{-1}\mathbf{Z}^{\top}\mathbf{W}\mathbf{r}.

(C.3)

We now bound the right-hand side in operator norm.
Using
Assumption
3.2

\big\|\mathbf{A}_{l}(\mathbf{Z}^{\top}\mathbf{W}\mathbf{Z})^{-1}\mathbf{A}_{l}^{\top}\big\|_{2}\leq\lambda_{0}^{-1},

we obtain

\displaystyle\Big\|\mathbb{E}\!\left[\hat{\bm{\theta}}_{\mathrm{DVCM}}(u_{0})-{\bm{\theta}}(u_{0})\mid\Gamma\right]\Big\|_{2}

\displaystyle\leq\big\|\mathbf{A}_{l}(\mathbf{Z}^{\top}\mathbf{W}\mathbf{Z})^{-1}\mathbf{A}_{l}^{\top}\big\|_{2}\;\big\|\mathbf{A}_{l}\mathbf{Z}^{\top}\mathbf{W}\mathbf{r}\big\|_{2}

\displaystyle\leq\lambda_{0}^{-1}\;\big\|\mathbf{A}_{l}\mathbf{Z}^{\top}\mathbf{W}\mathbf{r}\big\|_{2}.

(C.4)

Next, note that
\mathbf{A}_{l}\mathbf{Z}^{\top}
extracts the first
p
rows of
\mathbf{Z}^{\top}
, which correspond to the part containing
X_{ki}
only.
Equivalently,

\mathbf{A}_{l}\mathbf{Z}^{\top}\mathbf{W}\mathbf{r}=\sum_{k=0}^{K}\sum_{i\in\mathcal{I}_{k}}w_{k}\,X_{ki}\,r_{ki}=\sum_{k=0}^{K}\sum_{i\in\mathcal{I}_{k}}w_{k}\,X_{ki}\,X_{ki}^{\top}\Big\{{\bm{\theta}}(U_{k})-\sum_{v=0}^{l}{\bm{\theta}}^{(v)}(u_{0})(U_{k}-u_{0})^{v}/v!\Big\},

with
w_{k}:=W\!\Big(\frac{U_{k}-u_{0}}{h}\Big)/S_{h}
. Using
\|X_{ki}\|_{2}\leq 1
,

\big\|\mathbf{A}_{l}\mathbf{Z}^{\top}\mathbf{W}\mathbf{r}\big\|_{2}\leq\sum_{k=0}^{K}\sum_{i\in\mathcal{I}_{k}}w_{k}\Big\|{\bm{\theta}}(U_{k})-\sum_{v=0}^{l}{\bm{\theta}}^{(v)}(u_{0})(U_{k}-u_{0})^{v}/v!\Big\|_{2}.

Finally, by Assumption
3.1
(a) (
{\bm{\theta}}\in\mathcal{H}(\beta,L)
) and the standard local-polynomial remainder,
on the support of
w_{k}
we have
|U_{k}-u_{0}|\leq h
and hence

\Big\|{\bm{\theta}}(U_{k})-\sum_{v=0}^{l}{\bm{\theta}}^{(v)}(u_{0})(U_{k}-u_{0})^{v}/v!\Big\|_{2}\leq Ch^{\beta}.

Since
\sum_{k,i}w_{k}=1
(because they are normalized by
S_{h}
), it follows that

\big\|\mathbf{A}_{l}\mathbf{Z}^{\top}\mathbf{W}\mathbf{r}\big\|_{2}\leq Ch^{\beta}.

Plugging into (
C.4
) yields

\Big\|\mathbb{E}\!\left[\hat{\bm{\theta}}_{\mathrm{DVCM}}(u_{0})-{\bm{\theta}}(u_{0})\mid\Gamma\right]\Big\|_{2}\leq q_{1}^{\prime}h^{\beta},

for some constant
q_{1}^{\prime}>0
, and therefore

\mathbb{E}^{\top}\!\left[\hat{\bm{\theta}}_{\mathrm{DVCM}}(u_{0})-{\bm{\theta}}(u_{0})\mid\Gamma\right]A\,\mathbb{E}\!\left[\hat{\bm{\theta}}_{\mathrm{DVCM}}(u_{0})-{\bm{\theta}}(u_{0})\mid\Gamma\right]\leq\lambda_{A,1}{q_{1}^{\prime}}^{2}h^{2\beta}=:q_{1}h^{2\beta},

where
\lambda_{A,1}
is the largest eigenvalue of
A
.

Variance.
Let
{\bm{\epsilon}}
be the stacked noise vector with
\mathbb{E}[{\bm{\epsilon}}\mid\Gamma]=0
and

{\sf Var}({\bm{\epsilon}}\mid\Gamma)=\Sigma_{\epsilon}:={\sf diag}(\sigma^{2}(U_{k}))\preceq\sigma_{\max}^{2}I
.
Then

\hat{\bm{\theta}}_{\mathrm{DVCM}}(u_{0})-\mathbb{E}[\hat{\bm{\theta}}_{\mathrm{DVCM}}(u_{0})\mid\Gamma]=\mathbf{A}_{l}(\mathbf{Z}^{\top}\mathbf{W}\mathbf{Z})^{-1}\mathbf{Z}^{\top}\mathbf{W}{\bm{\epsilon}},

so

{\sf Cov}(\hat{\bm{\theta}}_{\mathrm{DVCM}}(u_{0})|\Gamma)=\mathbf{A}_{l}(\mathbf{Z}^{\top}\mathbf{W}\mathbf{Z})^{-1}\mathbf{Z}^{\top}\mathbf{W}\Sigma_{\epsilon}\mathbf{W}\mathbf{Z}(\mathbf{Z}^{\top}\mathbf{W}\mathbf{Z})^{-1}\mathbf{A}_{l}^{\top}\preceq\sigma_{\max}^{2}\,\mathbf{A}_{l}(\mathbf{Z}^{\top}\mathbf{W}\mathbf{Z})^{-1}\mathbf{Z}^{\top}\mathbf{W}^{2}\mathbf{Z}(\mathbf{Z}^{\top}\mathbf{W}\mathbf{Z})^{-1}\mathbf{A}_{l}^{\top}.

Because
W(u)=\frac{1}{2}\mathbf{1}(|u|\leq 1/2)
and
\mathbf{W}
is normalized by
S_{h}
, we have
\mathbf{W}^{2}=(2S_{h})^{-1}\mathbf{W}
, hence

(\mathbf{Z}^{\top}\mathbf{W}\mathbf{Z})^{-1}\mathbf{Z}^{\top}\mathbf{W}^{2}\mathbf{Z}(\mathbf{Z}^{\top}\mathbf{W}\mathbf{Z})^{-1}=\frac{1}{2S_{h}}(\mathbf{Z}^{\top}\mathbf{W}\mathbf{Z})^{-1}.

Therefore,

{\sf Cov}(\hat{\bm{\theta}}_{\mathrm{DVCM}}(u_{0})\mid\Gamma)\preceq\frac{\sigma_{\max}^{2}}{2S_{h}}\,\mathbf{A}_{l}(\mathbf{Z}^{\top}\mathbf{W}\mathbf{Z})^{-1}\mathbf{A}_{l}^{\top},

and

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\!\left[\big\|\hat{\bm{\theta}}_{\mathrm{DVCM}}(u_{0})-\mathbb{E}[\hat{\bm{\theta}}_{\mathrm{DVCM}}(u_{0})\mid\Gamma]\big\|_{A}^{2}\mid\Gamma\right]

\displaystyle={\sf tr}\!\big(A\,{\sf Cov}(\hat{\bm{\theta}}_{\mathrm{DVCM}}(u_{0})\mid\Gamma)\big)

\displaystyle\leq\frac{\sigma_{\max}^{2}}{2S_{h}}{\sf tr}\!\Big(A\,\mathbf{A}_{l}(\mathbf{Z}^{\top}\mathbf{W}\mathbf{Z})^{-1}\mathbf{A}_{l}^{\top}\Big)

\displaystyle\leq\frac{\sigma_{\max}^{2}\,\lambda_{A,1}}{2S_{h}}{\sf tr}\!\Big(\mathbf{A}_{l}(\mathbf{Z}^{\top}\mathbf{W}\mathbf{Z})^{-1}\mathbf{A}_{l}^{\top}\Big)

\displaystyle\leq\frac{\sigma_{\max}^{2}\,\lambda_{A,1}\,p}{2S_{h}}\big\|\mathbf{A}_{l}(\mathbf{Z}^{\top}\mathbf{W}\mathbf{Z})^{-1}\mathbf{A}_{l}^{\top}\big\|_{2}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{\sigma_{\max}^{2}\,\lambda_{A,1}\,p}{2\lambda_{0}}\,S_{h}^{-1}=:q_{2}S_{h}^{-1}.

Conclusion.
Combining the conditional squared bias and conditional variance bounds,

\mathbb{E}\!\left[\|\hat{\bm{\theta}}_{\mathrm{DVCM}}(u_{0})-{\bm{\theta}}(u_{0})\|_{A}^{2}\,\middle|\,\Gamma\right]\leq q_{1}h^{2\beta}+q_{2}S_{h}^{-1}.

C.6

Proof of Lemma
A.5

Fix
k\in\{0\}\cup[K]
and condition on
U_{k}=u_{k}
. Let

t_{k}\coloneqq\frac{u_{k}-u_{0}}{h},\qquad Z_{ki}\coloneqq{\bm{\Phi}}_{l}(t_{k})\otimes X_{ki},\qquad\eta_{ki}^{*}\coloneqq X_{ki}^{\top}{\bm{\theta}}(u_{k}),

and define

\delta_{ki}\coloneqq s_{1}\big(Z_{ki}^{\top}\bar{\bm{\theta}}(u_{0}),Y_{ki}\big)\,Z_{ki}\,W(t_{k}),\qquad\Delta_{k}\coloneqq n_{k}^{-1/2}\sum_{i\in\mathcal{I}_{k}}\delta_{ki}.

Throughout,
s_{j}(\eta,y)=\partial^{j}\ell(\eta,y)/\partial\eta^{j}
, and Assumptions

3.1
–
3.4
are assumed. We work with a natural exponential family and canonical link so that

\begin{split}&\mathbb{E}[s_{1}(\eta_{ki}^{*},Y_{ki})\mid X_{ki},U_{k}]=0\\
&\mathbb{E}[s_{1}^{2}(\eta_{ki}^{*},Y_{ki})\mid X_{ki},U_{k}]=\nu(u_{k})\,b^{\prime\prime}(\eta_{ki}^{*})\\
&s_{j}(\eta,Y_{ki})=b^{(j)}(\eta)\quad\text{for }j\geq 2,\text{ i.e. higher derivatives are independent of }Y,\end{split}

(C.5)

with variance function
\nu(\cdot)
bounded and continuous.

Conditional mean.
By Taylor expansion of
s_{1}(\cdot,Y_{ki})
in
\eta
around
\eta_{ki}^{*}
, write

s_{1}\big(Z_{ki}^{\top}\bar{\bm{\theta}}(u_{0}),Y_{ki}\big)=s_{1}(\eta_{ki}^{*},Y_{ki})+s_{2}(\eta_{ki}^{*},Y_{ki})\big(Z_{ki}^{\top}\bar{\bm{\theta}}(u_{0})-\eta_{ki}^{*}\big)+\frac{1}{2}\,s_{3}(\tilde{\eta}_{ki},Y_{ki})\big(Z_{ki}^{\top}\bar{\bm{\theta}}(u_{0})-\eta_{ki}^{*}\big)^{2},

(C.6)

where
\eta_{ki}^{*}=X_{ki}^{\top}{\bm{\theta}}(u_{k})
and
\tilde{\eta}_{ki}
lies between
\eta_{ki}^{*}
and
Z_{ki}^{\top}\bar{\bm{\theta}}(u_{0})
.
Using the identity
X_{ki}^{\top}=Z_{ki}^{\top}\mathbf{A}_{l}^{\top}
, the local–polynomial bias satisfies

\displaystyle Z_{ki}^{\top}\bar{\bm{\theta}}(u_{0})-\eta_{ki}^{*}

\displaystyle=X_{ki}^{\top}\frac{{\bm{\theta}}^{(l)}(u_{0})-{\bm{\theta}}^{(l)}(\tilde{u}_{k})}{l!}\,(u_{k}-u_{0})^{l}

\displaystyle=Z_{ki}^{\top}\mathbf{A}_{l}^{\top}\frac{{\bm{\theta}}^{(l)}(u_{0})-{\bm{\theta}}^{(l)}(\tilde{u}_{k})}{l!}\,(t_{k}h)^{l},

(C.7)

uniformly for
|t_{k}|
in the support of
W
(hence
|u_{0}{-}u_{k}|\leq h
), and
\tilde{u}_{k}
is between
u_{k}
and
u_{0}
. Moreover, when
|u_{0}{-}u_{k}|\leq h
, by Hölder continuity
\mathcal{H}(L,\beta)
and boundedness of
\|X_{ki}\|_{2}
, its absolute value is such that

\displaystyle|Z_{ki}^{\top}\bar{\bm{\theta}}(u_{0})-\eta_{ki}^{*}|\leq\|X_{ki}\|_{2}\frac{|{\bm{\theta}}^{(l)}(u_{0})-{\bm{\theta}}^{(l)}(\tilde{u}_{k})|}{l!}\,|u_{k}-u_{0}|^{l}=O(h^{\beta})\,.

(C.8)

Taking
\mathbb{E}[\cdot\mid X_{ki},U_{k}{=}u_{k}]
in (
C.6
) and using the identity

s_{2}(\eta_{ki}^{*},Y_{ki})=b^{\prime\prime}(\eta_{ki}^{*})
gives

\mathbb{E}\Big[s_{1}\big(Z_{ki}^{\top}\bar{\bm{\theta}}(u_{0}),Y_{ki}\big)\,\Big|\,X_{ki},U_{k}{=}u_{k}\Big]=b^{\prime\prime}(\eta_{ki}^{*})\big(Z_{ki}^{\top}\bar{\bm{\theta}}(u_{0})-\eta_{ki}^{*}\big)+O\big(|Z_{ki}^{\top}\bar{\bm{\theta}}(u_{0})-\eta_{ki}^{*}|^{2}\big).

(C.9)

Multiplying (
C.9
) by
Z_{ki}W(t_{k})
and taking
\mathbb{E}[\cdot\mid U_{k}{=}u_{k}]
, we use

\mathbb{E}[b^{\prime\prime}(\eta_{ki}^{*})X_{ki}X_{ki}^{\top}\mid U_{k}=u_{k}]=\Psi(u_{k})
and (
C.7
) to obtain

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}[\delta_{ki}\mid U_{k}=u_{k}]

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}\left[b^{\prime\prime}(\eta_{ki}^{*})Z_{ki}\big(Z_{ki}^{\top}\bar{\bm{\theta}}(u_{0})-\eta_{ki}^{*}\big)W(t_{k})\mid U_{k}=u_{k}\right]+R_{k}

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}\left[b^{\prime\prime}(\eta_{ki}^{*})Z_{ki}Z_{ki}^{\top}\mathbf{A}_{l}^{\top}\frac{{\bm{\theta}}^{(l)}(u_{0})-{\bm{\theta}}^{(l)}(\tilde{u}_{k})}{l!}\,(t_{k}h)^{l}W(t_{k})\mid U_{k}=u_{k}\right]+R_{k}

\displaystyle={\bm{\Phi}}_{l}(t_{k})^{\otimes 2}\otimes\Psi(u_{k})\mathbf{A}_{l}^{\top}\frac{{\bm{\theta}}^{(l)}(u_{0})-{\bm{\theta}}^{(l)}(\tilde{u}_{k})}{l!}\,(t_{k}h)^{l}\,W(t_{k})+R_{k},

where the remainder satisfies

R_{k}=\mathbb{E}\Big[\,O\big(|Z_{ki}^{\top}\bar{\bm{\theta}}(u_{0})-\eta_{ki}^{*}|^{2}\big)\,\|Z_{ki}\|\,\Big|\,U_{k}{=}u_{k}\Big]\,W(t_{k})=O(h^{2\beta})=o(h^{\beta}),

by boundedness of
\|X_{ki}\|_{2}
, result in (
C.8
), and the bounded–moment assumption on
b^{(3)}
(so that
\mathbb{E}[|s_{3}(\tilde{\eta}_{ki},Y_{ki})|^{4}\mid U_{k}]
is
O(1)
).
Therefore, it follows that

n_{k}^{-1/2}\,\mathbb{E}[\Delta_{k}\mid U_{k}{=}u_{k}]={\bm{\Phi}}_{l}(t_{k})^{\otimes 2}\otimes\Psi(u_{k})\,\mathbf{A}_{l}^{\top}\frac{{\bm{\theta}}^{(l)}(u_{0})-{\bm{\theta}}^{(l)}(\tilde{u}_{k})}{l!}\,(t_{k}h)^{l}\,W(t_{k})\{1+o(1)\}

Moreover, by bounded spectral norm of
{\bm{\Phi}}_{l}(t_{k})^{\otimes 2},\Psi(u_{k})
,
\mathbf{A}_{l}
, and the same argument as in (
C.8
), its absolute value is such that

\displaystyle\Big|n_{k}^{-1/2}\,\mathbb{E}[\Delta_{k}\mid U_{k}{=}u_{k}]\Big|

\displaystyle\leq\Big\|{\bm{\Phi}}_{l}(t_{k})^{\otimes 2}\otimes\Psi(u_{k})\,\mathbf{A}_{l}^{\top}\frac{{\bm{\theta}}^{(l)}(u_{0})-{\bm{\theta}}^{(l)}(\tilde{u}_{k})}{l!}\,(t_{k}h)^{l}\,W(t_{k})\Big\|_{2}\,\{1+o(1)\}

\displaystyle=O(h^{\beta})\,.

Conditional variance.
By the same expansion as in Equation (
C.6
) and we square both sides to obtain

s_{1}^{2}\big(Z_{ki}^{\top}\bar{\bm{\theta}}(u_{0}),Y_{ki}\big)=s_{1}^{2}(\eta_{ki}^{*},Y_{ki})+O(h^{\beta})

The second identity in (
C.5
) further implies

\mathbb{E}\Big[s_{1}^{2}\big(Z_{ki}^{\top}\bar{\bm{\theta}}(u_{0}),Y_{ki}\big)\,\Big|\,X_{ki},U_{k}=u_{k}\Big]=\nu(u_{k})\,b^{\prime\prime}(\eta_{ki}^{*})+O(h^{\beta}),

where the
O(h^{\beta})
term collects all the higher-order terms. Hence

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\big[\delta_{ki}\delta_{ki}^{\top}\mid U_{k}=u_{k}\big]

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}\big[s_{1}^{2}\big(Z_{ki}^{\top}\bar{\bm{\theta}}(u_{0}),Y_{ki}\big)Z_{ki}Z_{ki}^{\top}\mid U_{k}=u_{k}\big]\,W(t_{k})^{2}

\displaystyle=\nu(u_{k})\,\Big({\bm{\Phi}}_{l}(t_{k})^{\otimes 2}\otimes\Psi(u_{k})\Big)\,W(t_{k})^{2}+O(h^{\beta}).

Since
\mathbb{E}[\delta_{ki}\mid U_{k}=u_{k}]=O(h^{\beta})
, the subtraction of

\mathbb{E}[\delta_{ki}\mid U_{k}=u_{k}]\,\mathbb{E}[\delta_{ki}\mid U_{k}=u_{k}]^{\top}
affects the
variance only at order
O(h^{2\beta})
, so

{\sf Var}[\delta_{ki}\mid U_{k}=u_{k}]=\nu(u_{k})\,\Big({\bm{\Phi}}_{l}(t_{k})^{\otimes 2}\otimes\Psi(u_{k})\Big)\,W(t_{k})^{2}+O(h^{\beta}).

Because
\{\delta_{ki}\}_{i\in\mathcal{I}_{k}}
are conditionally i.i.d.,

{\sf Var}[\Delta_{k}\mid U_{k}=u_{k}]={\sf Var}[\delta_{ki}\mid U_{k}=u_{k}]=\nu(u_{k})\,\Big({\bm{\Phi}}_{l}(t_{k})^{\otimes 2}\otimes\Psi(u_{k})\Big)\,W(t_{k})^{2}+O(h^{\beta}).

The matrix term
\Lambda_{k}
.
Recall

\Lambda_{k}=n_{k}^{-1}\sum_{i\in\mathcal{I}_{k}}s_{2}\big(Z_{ki}^{\top}\bar{\bm{\theta}}(u_{0}),Y_{ki}\big)\,Z_{ki}Z_{ki}^{\top}\,W(t_{k}).

By a first–order Taylor expansion of
s_{2}(\cdot,Y_{ki})
in
\eta
around
\eta_{ki}^{*}
,

s_{2}\big(Z_{ki}^{\top}\bar{\bm{\theta}}(u_{0}),Y_{ki}\big)=s_{2}(\eta_{ki}^{*},Y_{ki})+s_{3}(\tilde{\eta}_{ki},Y_{ki})\,\big(Z_{ki}^{\top}\bar{\bm{\theta}}(u_{0})-\eta_{ki}^{*}\big),

where
\tilde{\eta}_{ki}
lies between
\eta_{ki}^{*}
and
Z_{ki}^{\top}\bar{\bm{\theta}}(u_{0})
.
Taking
\mathbb{E}[\cdot\mid X_{ki},U_{k}=u_{k}]
and using the third identity in (
C.5
) gives

\mathbb{E}\big[s_{2}\big(Z_{ki}^{\top}\bar{\bm{\theta}}(u_{0}),Y_{ki}\big)\mid X_{ki},U_{k}=u_{k}\big]=b^{\prime\prime}(\eta_{ki}^{*})+\mathbb{E}\big[s_{3}(\tilde{\eta}_{ki},Y_{ki})\mid X_{ki},U_{k}=u_{k}\big]\,\big(Z_{ki}^{\top}\bar{\bm{\theta}}(u_{0})-\eta_{ki}^{*}\big).

By the same local polynomial approximation in (
C.7
),

Z_{ki}^{\top}\bar{\bm{\theta}}(u_{0})-\eta_{ki}^{*}=O(h^{\,\beta})
uniformly for

|t_{k}|
in the support of
W
. Now we marginalize
X_{ki}
. By the moment condition in Assumption
2′′
,

\mathbb{E}[\,|s_{3}(\tilde{\eta}_{ki},Y_{ki})|\mid U_{k}=u_{k}]=\mathbb{E}[|b^{(3)}(\tilde{\eta}_{ki})|\mid U_{k}=u_{k}]=O(1)\,.

Hence

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}[\Lambda_{k}\mid U_{k}=u_{k}]

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}\Big[\big(b^{\prime\prime}(\eta_{ki}^{*})+O(h^{\,\beta})\big)\,Z_{ki}Z_{ki}^{\top}\,\Big|\,U_{k}=u_{k}\Big]\,W(t_{k})

\displaystyle=\Big({\bm{\Phi}}_{l}(t_{k})^{\otimes 2}\otimes\mathbb{E}[\,b^{\prime\prime}(\eta_{ki}^{*})\,X_{ki}X_{ki}^{\top}\mid U_{k}=u_{k}]\Big)\,W(t_{k})+O(h^{\,\beta})

\displaystyle=\Big({\bm{\Phi}}_{l}(t_{k})^{\otimes 2}\otimes\Psi(u_{k})\Big)\,W(t_{k})+O(h^{\,\beta}).

C.7

Proof of Proposition
A.9

Proof C.1

We first note that the condition

\frac{\gamma}{K}\ll h\lesssim\Big(\frac{\gamma}{n}\Big)^{\frac{1}{2\beta+1}}
implies a relationship we will repeatedly apply:

\frac{h^{2\beta-1}}{K}\ll\frac{h^{2\beta}}{\gamma}\lesssim\frac{1}{nh}\,.

(C.10)

Let
s_{j}(\eta,y)=\partial^{j}\ell(\eta,y)/\partial\eta^{j}
denote derivatives w.r.t. the first argument of
\ell
. Recall

\hat{\bm{\theta}}_{\mathrm{GDVCM}}(u_{0})=\mathbf{A}_{l}\hat{\bm{\alpha}}_{\mathrm{GDVCM}},\qquad\hat{\bm{\alpha}}_{\mathrm{GDVCM}}=\argmin_{{\bm{\alpha}}\in\mathbb{R}^{(l+1)p}}\sum_{k=0}^{K}\sum_{i\in\mathcal{I}_{k}}\ell\big(Z_{ki}^{\top}{\bm{\alpha}},Y_{ki}\big)\,W\left(\frac{U_{k}-u_{0}}{h}\right),

with
\mathbf{A}_{l}=[I_{p},\,0,\ldots,0]
,
Z_{ki}={\bm{\Phi}}_{l}(t_{k})\otimes X_{ki}
and
t_{k}=(u_{k}-u_{0})/h
.
Write

\bar{\bm{\theta}}(u_{0})^{\top}=\big[{\bm{\theta}}(u_{0})^{\top},\,h{\bm{\theta}}^{\prime}(u_{0})^{\top},\,\ldots,\,h^{l}{\bm{\theta}}^{(l)}(u_{0})^{\top}\big],\qquad r_{n}\coloneqq\left(\frac{nh}{\gamma}\right)^{-1/2}.

We also use short hand notation for the density of
U_{k}
:

f_{\gamma}(u)\coloneqq\frac{1}{\gamma}f\left(\frac{u-u^{*}}{\gamma}\right)\,.

Due to Assumption
3.3
, we assume w.l.o.g. that

p_{k}\coloneqq\frac{n_{k}}{n}\asymp\frac{1}{K}.

The norm
\|\cdot\|
used in the proof denotes the
\ell_{2}
-norm when applied to a vector, and the spectral norm when applied to a matrix.

Taylor Expansion.

Define

Q_{n}(\alpha)\coloneqq\sum_{k=0}^{K}\sum_{i\in\mathcal{I}_{k}}\ell\big(Z_{ki}^{\top}\alpha,Y_{ki}\big)\,W(t_{k}),\qquad D_{n}(\delta)\coloneqq Q_{n}\big(\bar{\bm{\theta}}(u_{0})+r_{n}\delta\big)-Q_{n}(\bar{\bm{\theta}}(u_{0})),

where
r_{n}=(nh/\gamma)^{-1/2}
. Thus the minimizer
\hat{\delta}
of
D_{n}(\delta)
satisfy
\hat{\delta}=r_{n}^{-1}(\hat{\bm{\alpha}}_{\mathrm{GDVCM}}-\bar{\bm{\theta}}(u_{0}))
.
A second-order Taylor expansion of each summand at
\eta_{ki}^{*}\coloneqq Z_{ki}^{\top}\bar{\bm{\theta}}(u_{0})
yields

\displaystyle\ell(\eta_{ki}^{*}+r_{n}Z_{ki}^{\top}\delta,Y_{ki})

\displaystyle=\ell(\eta_{ki}^{*},Y_{ki})+r_{n}s_{1}(\eta_{ki}^{*},Y_{ki})Z_{ki}^{\top}\delta

\displaystyle\quad+\frac{1}{2}r_{n}^{2}s_{2}(\eta_{ki}^{*},Y_{ki})(Z_{ki}^{\top}\delta)^{2}+\frac{1}{6}r_{n}^{3}s_{3}(\tilde{\eta}_{ki},Y_{ki})(Z_{ki}^{\top}\delta)^{3},

(C.11)

with
\tilde{\eta}_{ki}
between
\eta_{ki}^{*}
and
\eta_{ki}^{*}+r_{n}Z_{ki}^{\top}\delta
. Define the following quantities

\bar{\Delta}\coloneqq-\frac{1}{nh}\sum_{k=0}^{K}n_{k}^{1/2}\Delta_{k},\quad\bar{\Lambda}\coloneqq\frac{1}{nh}\sum_{k=0}^{K}n_{k}\Lambda_{k},\quad R_{n}(\delta)=\frac{1}{6}\,r_{n}^{3}\sum_{k=0}^{K}\sum_{i\in\mathcal{I}_{k}}s_{3}(\tilde{\eta}_{ki},Y_{ki})\,(Z_{ki}^{\top}\delta)^{3}\,W(t_{k}),

with (as in Lemma
A.5
)

\Delta_{k}\coloneqq n_{k}^{-1/2}\sum_{i\in\mathcal{I}_{k}}s_{1}\big(Z_{ki}^{\top}\bar{\bm{\theta}}(u_{0}),Y_{ki}\big)\,Z_{ki}\,W(t_{k}),\quad\Lambda_{k}\coloneqq n_{k}^{-1}\sum_{i\in\mathcal{I}_{k}}s_{2}\big(Z_{ki}^{\top}\bar{\bm{\theta}}(u_{0}),Y_{ki}\big)\,Z_{ki}Z_{ki}^{\top}\,W(t_{k}).

Summing over
(k,i)
for Equation (
C.1
) and collecting terms gives

D_{n}(\delta)=-(nh)\,r_{n}\,\delta^{\top}\bar{\Delta}+\frac{1}{2}(nh)\,r_{n}^{2}\,\delta^{\top}\bar{\Lambda}\,\delta+R_{n}(\delta).

(C.12)

Marginalizing
U_{k}
.

From Lemma
A.5
, for each
k
and small enough
h
,

\displaystyle n_{k}^{-1/2}\,\mathbb{E}[\Delta_{k}\mid U_{k}=u_{k}]

\displaystyle={\bm{\Phi}}_{l}(t_{k})^{\otimes 2}\otimes\Psi(u_{k})\,\mathbf{A}_{l}^{\top}\frac{{\bm{\theta}}^{(l)}(u_{0})-{\bm{\theta}}^{(l)}(\tilde{u}_{k})}{l!}\,(t_{k}h)^{l}\,W(t_{k})\{1+o(1)\}\lesssim h^{\beta},

(C.13)

\displaystyle{\sf Var}[\Delta_{k}\mid U_{k}=u_{k}]

\displaystyle=\nu(u_{k})\big({\bm{\Phi}}_{l}(t_{k})^{\otimes 2}\otimes\Psi(u_{k})\big)W(t_{k})^{2}+O(h^{\beta}),

(C.14)

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}[\Lambda_{k}\mid U_{k}=u_{k}]

\displaystyle=\big({\bm{\Phi}}_{l}(t_{k})^{\otimes 2}\otimes\Psi(u_{k})\big)W(t_{k})+O(h^{\beta}).

(C.15)

1) Mean of
\bar{\Delta}
.

Since
\bar{\Delta}=-(nh)^{-1}\sum_{k=0}^{K}n_{k}^{1/2}\Delta_{k}
,

\mathbb{E}\bar{\Delta}=-\sum_{k=0}^{K}p_{k}\Big\{\,h^{-1}n_{k}^{-1/2}\,\mathbb{E}\Delta_{k}\,\Big\}.

For
k\in[K]
, integrating (
C.13
) w.r.t. the density
f_{\gamma}(u)=\gamma^{-1}f\Big(\tfrac{u-u^{*}}{\gamma}\Big)
of
U_{k}

and writing
t=(u-u_{0})/h
,

\displaystyle h^{-1}n_{k}^{-1/2}\,\mathbb{E}\Delta_{k}

\displaystyle=h^{-1}\int\big({\bm{\Phi}}_{l}(t)^{\otimes 2}\otimes\Psi(u)\big)\,\mathbf{A}_{l}^{\top}\frac{{\bm{\theta}}^{(l)}(u_{0})-{\bm{\theta}}^{(l)}(\tilde{u}_{k})}{l!}\,(th)^{l}W(t)f_{\gamma}(u)\,du\,(1+o(1))

\displaystyle\overset{t=(u-u_{0})/h}{=}\int{\bm{\Phi}}_{l}(t)^{\otimes 2}t^{l}W(t)\ \otimes\ \Psi(u_{0})\mathbf{A}_{l}^{\top}\bigg[\frac{{\bm{\theta}}^{(l)}(u_{0})-{\bm{\theta}}^{(l)}(\tilde{u}_{k})}{l!}\ h^{\,l}\,\gamma^{-1}\bigg]f\Big(\tfrac{u_{0}-u^{*}+ht}{\gamma}\Big)\,dt\,(1+o(1)).

Notice that due to Hölder continuity and the fact that
|\tilde{u}_{k}-u_{0}|\leq h
, it holds that

\|{\bm{\theta}}^{(l)}(u_{0})-{\bm{\theta}}^{(l)}(\tilde{u}_{k})\|\lesssim h^{\beta-l}

and thus

h^{-1}n_{k}^{-1/2}\,\mathbb{E}\Delta_{k}=O(\gamma^{-1}h^{\beta})\quad\implies\quad\mathbb{E}\bar{\Delta}=O(\gamma^{-1}h^{\beta}).

(C.16)

2) Variance of
\bar{\Delta}
.

By the law of total variance,

{\sf Var}(\bar{\Delta})=(nh)^{-2}\sum_{k=0}^{K}n_{k}\,{\sf Var}(\Delta_{k})=(nh)^{-2}\sum_{k=0}^{K}n_{k}\left\{\mathbb{E}\big[{\sf Var}(\Delta_{k}\mid U_{k})\big]+{\sf Var}\big(\mathbb{E}[\Delta_{k}\mid U_{k}]\big)\right\}.

For the leading term, using (
C.14
) and the change of variables

t=(u-u_{0})/h
,

\displaystyle h^{-1}\,\mathbb{E}\big[{\sf Var}(\Delta_{k}\mid U_{k})\big]

\displaystyle=h^{-1}\int\nu(u)\,\big({\bm{\Phi}}_{l}(t)^{\otimes 2}\otimes\Psi(u)\big)\,W(t)^{2}\,f_{\gamma}(u)\,du\,(1+o(1))

\displaystyle=\gamma^{-1}\Big[\textstyle\int{\bm{\Phi}}_{l}(t)^{\otimes 2}W(t)^{2}\,f\Big(\tfrac{u_{0}-u^{*}+ht}{\gamma}\Big)dt\Big]\otimes\Psi(u_{0})\nu(u_{0})\,(1+o(1))

\displaystyle=\gamma^{-1}\zeta_{0,2}\otimes\Psi(u_{0})\nu(u_{0})\,(1+o(1)).

Hence

(nh)^{-2}\sum_{k=0}^{K}n_{k}\,\mathbb{E}\big[{\sf Var}(\Delta_{k}\mid U_{k})\big]=(\gamma nh)^{-1}\,\nu(u_{0})\,\zeta_{0,2}\otimes\Psi(u_{0})\,(1+o(1)).

For the second term, by the bound
\|{\sf Var}(Z)\|\leq\mathbb{E}\|Z\|^{2}
and (
C.13
),
together with the same change of variables
t=(u-u_{0})/h
and the upper-boundedness
of
\|{\bm{\theta}}^{(l)}(u_{0})-{\bm{\theta}}^{(l)}(\tilde{u}_{k})\|
,
\lambda_{\max}\{\Psi(u)\}
, and
f(u)
,

\displaystyle\big\|{\sf Var}\big(\mathbb{E}[\Delta_{k}\mid U_{k}]\big)\big\|

\displaystyle\lesssim n_{k}\,h^{2\beta}\int\|{\bm{\Phi}}_{l}(t)^{\otimes 2}\|^{2}\,t^{2l}W(t)^{2}\,f_{\gamma}(u_{0}+ht)\,du

\displaystyle=\gamma^{-1}n_{k}\,h^{2\beta+1}\int\|{\bm{\Phi}}_{l}(t)^{\otimes 2}\|^{2}\,t^{2l}W(t)^{2}\,f((u_{0}+ht)/\gamma)\,dt

Since
{\bm{\Phi}}_{l}(t)=(1,t,\ldots,t^{l}/l!)^{\top}
implies

\|{\bm{\Phi}}_{l}(t)\|_{2}^{2}\mathbf{1}(|t|\leq 1)\lesssim 1
and
W
is the uniform kernel
(Assumption
3.4
) with finite moments, we obtain

\big\|{\sf Var}\big(\mathbb{E}[\Delta_{k}\mid U_{k}]\big)\big\|\lesssim\frac{n_{k}\,h^{2\beta+1}}{\gamma}.

Therefore,

(nh)^{-2}\sum_{k=0}^{K}n_{k}\,\big\|{\sf Var}\big(\mathbb{E}[\Delta_{k}\mid U_{k}]\big)\big\|\lesssim(nh)^{-2}\sum_{k=0}^{K}\frac{n_{k}^{2}\,h^{2\beta+1}}{\gamma}=\frac{h^{2\beta-1}}{\gamma}\sum_{k=0}^{K}\Big(\frac{n_{k}}{n}\Big)^{2}\lesssim\frac{h^{2\beta-1}}{\gamma K},

using
n_{k}/n\lesssim 1/K
.
Thus combining the two components in the law of total variance and applying Equation (
C.10
) yield:

{\sf Var}(\bar{\Delta})=(\gamma nh)^{-1}\,\nu(u_{0})\,\zeta_{0,2}\otimes\Psi(u_{0})\,(1+o(1)).

(C.17)

3) Mean of
\bar{\Lambda}
.

Since
\bar{\Lambda}=(nh)^{-1}\sum_{k=0}^{K}n_{k}\Lambda_{k}
,

\mathbb{E}\bar{\Lambda}=\sum_{k=0}^{K}p_{k}\Big\{\,h^{-1}\mathbb{E}\Lambda_{k}\,\Big\}.

Integrating (
C.15
) as above gives

\displaystyle h^{-1}\mathbb{E}\Lambda_{k}

\displaystyle=h^{-1}\int\big({\bm{\Phi}}_{l}(t)^{\otimes 2}\otimes\Psi(u)\big)\,W(t)f_{\gamma}(u)\,du\,(1+o(1))

\displaystyle\overset{t=(u-u_{0})/h}{=}\gamma^{-1}\left[\int{\bm{\Phi}}_{l}(t)^{\otimes 2}W(t)f\Big(\tfrac{u_{0}-u^{*}+ht}{\gamma}\Big)\,dt\right]\otimes\Psi(u_{0})\,(1+o(1))

\displaystyle=\gamma^{-1}\zeta_{0,1}\ \otimes\ \Psi(u_{0})\,(1+o(1)).

Therefore,

\mathbb{E}\bar{\Lambda}=\gamma^{-1}\zeta_{0,1}\otimes\Psi(u_{0})\,(1+o(1)).

(C.18)

Rate of minimizer.

Fix
M<\infty
and consider the sphere
\{\|\delta\|=M\}
.
Assumption
2′′
implies bounded
\mathbb{E}|s_{3}(\cdot,Y)|^{4}=\mathbb{E}|b^{(3)}(\cdot)|^{4}
and
\mathbb{E}\|X\|^{4}
, while Assumption
3.4
(compactly supported bounded
W
) implies
\mathbb{E}[W(t_{k})]\asymp\int\mathbf{1}(|u|\leq h)f_{\gamma}(u)du=O(h/\gamma)
.
Using the Taylor remainder above and
|Z_{ki}^{\top}\delta|\leq\|Z_{ki}\|\,\|\delta\|
, we get

\sup_{\|\delta\|\leq M}|R_{n}(\delta)|=O_{p}(1)\cdot M^{3}\,r_{n}^{3}\sum_{k=0}^{K}\sum_{i\in\mathcal{I}_{k}}\|Z_{ki}\|^{3}W(t_{k})=O_{p}\Big(M^{3}\,r_{n}^{3}\,\frac{nh}{\gamma}\Big)=O_{p}\Big(\frac{M^{3}}{\sqrt{nh/\gamma}}\Big)=o_{p}(1).

(C.19)

Equation (
C.18
) implies

\gamma\bar{\Lambda}\xrightarrow{p}\zeta_{0,1}\otimes\Psi(u_{0})
, so for some
c>0
,

\lambda_{\min}(\bar{\Lambda})\ \geq\ \frac{c}{\gamma}\qquad\text{w.p.}\to 1.

Moreover, from Equations (
C.16
) and (
C.17
), as well as condition
nh^{2\beta+1}/\gamma\lesssim 1
,

\|\bar{\Delta}\|=O_{p}\big((nh\,\gamma)^{-1/2}\big).

Recall the decomposition in (
C.12
):

D_{n}(\delta)=-(nh)\,r_{n}\,\delta^{\top}\bar{\Delta}+\frac{1}{2}\,(nh)\,r_{n}^{2}\,\delta^{\top}\bar{\Lambda}\,\delta+R_{n}(\delta).

Hence, on
\{\|\delta\|=M\}
,

\displaystyle\inf_{\|\delta\|=M}D_{n}(\delta)

\displaystyle\geq-(nh)\,r_{n}\,\|\bar{\Delta}\|\,M+\frac{1}{2}\,(nh)\,r_{n}^{2}\,\lambda_{\min}(\bar{\Lambda})\,M^{2}-\sup_{\|\delta\|\leq M}|R_{n}(\delta)|

\displaystyle\geq-\,M\cdot O_{p}(1)+\frac{1}{2}\,\gamma\cdot\frac{c}{\gamma}\,M^{2}-o_{p}(1)=\frac{1}{2}\,c\,M^{2}-O_{p}(M)-o_{p}(1).

Choosing
M
as a sufficiently large constant makes the RHS positive w.p.
\to 1
.
Since
D_{n}(0)=0
and
D_{n}
is convex, the (unique) minimizer

\hat{\delta}\coloneqq\arg\min_{\delta}D_{n}(\delta)
lies in the ball
\{\|\delta\|\leq M\}
w.p.
\to 1
, which justifies the rate
r_{n}
and consistency.

Linearization.

On
\{\|\delta\|\leq M\}
we have the uniform expansion, hence

\nabla D_{n}(\delta)=-(nh)\,r_{n}\bar{\Delta}+(nh)\,r_{n}^{2}\,\bar{\Lambda}\,\delta+\nabla R_{n}(\delta).

\nabla D_{n}(\hat{\delta})=0
, so

\hat{\delta}=r_{n}^{-1}\bar{\Lambda}^{-1}\bar{\Delta}-\gamma^{-1}\bar{\Lambda}^{-1}\nabla R_{n}(\hat{\delta})=r_{n}^{-1}\bar{\Lambda}^{-1}\bar{\Delta}+o_{p}(1),

because
\sup_{\|\delta\|\leq M}\|\nabla R_{n}(\delta)\|=o_{p}(1)
and
\|\bar{\Lambda}^{-1}\|=O_{p}(\gamma)
by Equations (
C.19
) and (
C.18
).
Finally, since
\hat{\bm{\alpha}}_{\mathrm{GDVCM}}=\bar{\bm{\theta}}(u_{0})+r_{n}\hat{\delta}
and
\hat{\bm{\theta}}_{\mathrm{GDVCM}}(u_{0})=\mathbf{A}_{l}\hat{\bm{\alpha}}_{\mathrm{GDVCM}}
,

\mathbf{A}_{l}\hat{\delta}=r_{n}^{-1}\big(\hat{\bm{\theta}}_{\mathrm{GDVCM}}(u_{0})-{\bm{\theta}}(u_{0})\big)=r_{n}^{-1}\mathbf{A}_{l}\,\bar{\Lambda}^{-1}\bar{\Delta}+o_{p}(1).

(C.20)

CLT for
\bar{\Delta}
.

Fix a unit vector
v_{0}\in\mathbb{R}^{p(l+1)}
and define

T_{K}(v_{0})\coloneqq v_{0}^{\top}(\bar{\Delta}-\mathbb{E}\bar{\Delta})=-\frac{1}{nh}\sum_{k=0}^{K}n_{k}^{1/2}\,\eta_{k}(v_{0}),\qquad\eta_{k}(v_{0})\coloneqq v_{0}^{\top}\big(\Delta_{k}-\mathbb{E}\Delta_{k}\big).

By construction,
\{\eta_{k}(v_{0})\}_{k=0}^{K}
are independent, centered random variables. From
(
C.14
) and Lemma
A.5
,

{\sf Var}\big(\eta_{k}(v_{0})\mid U_{k}=u_{k}\big)=v_{0}^{\top}\Big(\nu(u_{k})\,{\bm{\Phi}}_{l}(t_{k})^{\otimes 2}\otimes\Psi(u_{k})\Big)v_{0}\,W(t_{k})^{2}+O(h^{\beta})\,.

Thus, taking expectation w.r.t.
U_{k}
over density
f_{\gamma}(u)=\gamma^{-1}f(\gamma^{-1}(u-u^{*}))
yields

{\sf Var}\big(T_{K}(v_{0})\big)=(nh)^{-2}\sum_{k=0}^{K}n_{k}\,{\sf Var}\big(\eta_{k}(v_{0})\big)=(\gamma nh)^{-1}\,\nu(u_{0})v_{0}^{\top}(\zeta_{0,2}\otimes\Psi(u_{0}))v_{0}\,(1+o(1)).

Moreover, since
n_{k}\asymp n/K
, it follows that

\mathbb{E}\left[\sum_{k}n_{k}^{3/2}W(t_{k})\right]\asymp(Kh/\gamma)\,(n/K)^{3/2}\,.

Assumption
2′′
(bounded moments of
Y
) implies the term

\mathbb{E}|\eta_{k}(v_{0})|^{3}
is upper bounded.
Hence the Lyapunov condition is verified:

\frac{\sum_{k=0}^{K}\mathbb{E}\left[\left|\frac{n_{k}^{1/2}}{nh}\eta_{k}(v_{0})\right|^{3}\right]}{{\sf Var}(T_{K}(v_{0}))^{3/2}}\lesssim\frac{(Kh/\gamma)\,(n/K)^{3/2}/(nh)^{3}}{\big((nh)^{-1}\cdot\gamma^{-1}\big)^{3/2}}\asymp\sqrt{\frac{\gamma}{Kh}}\ \longrightarrow\ 0\,.

Therefore, by Lyapunov’s CLT,

\frac{T_{K}(v_{0})}{\sqrt{{\sf Var}(T_{K}(v_{0}))}}\ \xrightarrow{d}\ \mathcal{N}(0,1).

By the Cramér–Wold device,

\sqrt{\gamma nh}\big(\bar{\Delta}-\mathbb{E}\bar{\Delta}\big)\ \xrightarrow{d}\ \mathcal{N}\left(0,\ \zeta_{0,2}\otimes\Psi(u_{0})\nu(u_{0})\right).

(C.21)

Collecting results.

Combine Equations (
C.16
), (
C.18
), and (
C.21
). The bias term is

\mathbf{b}_{\mathrm{GDVCM}}(u_{0})=\mathbf{A}_{l}(\mathbb{E}\bar{\Lambda})^{-1}\mathbb{E}\bar{\Delta}=O(h^{\beta})

and the asymptotic variance of the centered estimator is

\Omega_{\mathrm{GDVCM}}(u_{0})=\nu(u_{0})\,\mathbf{e}_{1,l+1}^{\top}\,\zeta_{0,1}^{-1}\zeta_{0,2}\zeta_{0,1}^{-1}\,\mathbf{e}_{1,l+1}\ \otimes\ \Psi(u_{0})^{-1}.

Therefore,

\sqrt{\frac{nh}{\gamma}}\Big(\hat{\bm{\theta}}_{\mathrm{GDVCM}}(u_{0})-{\bm{\theta}}(u_{0})-\mathbf{b}_{\mathrm{GDVCM}}(u_{0})\Big)\ \xrightarrow{d}\mathcal{N}\big(0,\ \Omega_{\mathrm{GDVCM}}(u_{0})\big).

C.8

Proof of Proposition
A.8

Proof C.2

The asymptotic distribution for the linear DVCM is a
special case
of the generalized DVCM result (see Appendix
C.7
for the proof under a more general setup). Below, we highlight only the differences from the general proof. Recall that the model is:

Y_{ki}=X_{ki}^{\top}{\bm{\theta}}(U_{k})+\varepsilon_{ki},\quad\mathbb{E}[\varepsilon_{ki}\mid X_{ki},U_{k}]=0,\quad{\sf Var}(\varepsilon_{ki}\mid X_{ki},U_{k})=\sigma^{2}(U_{k})\,.

For linear regression, our loss function
\ell(\eta,y)
is the squared-error loss, i.e.
\ell(\eta,y)=(y-\eta)^{2}/2
, where
\eta=X^{\top}\theta
. As a consequence, the derivatives of the loss function (w.r.t.
\eta
) satisfy:

s_{1}(\eta,y)=\eta-y,\quad s_{2}(\eta,y)=1,\quad s_{3}(\eta,y)=0\,.

Furthermore, we have
b(x)=x^{2}/2
, which implies
b^{\prime}(x)=x,b^{\prime\prime}(x)=1
and
b^{\prime\prime\prime}(x)=0
.

Main differences between the two proofs.

In case of linear regression, the term with
s_{3}
in the proof of Proposition
A.9
vanishes as
s_{3}\equiv 0
. Furthermore, the definition of the conditional variance
\Psi(u)
simplifies to:

\Psi(u)=\mathbb{E}\left[XX^{\top}b^{\prime\prime}(X^{\top}{\bm{\theta}}(u))\mid U=u\right]=\mathbb{E}[XX^{\top}\mid U=u]\,,

and the variance term
\nu(u)
becomes
\sigma^{2}(u)
.

With the same idea in the proof of Proposition
A.9
, we construct the function
D_{n}(\delta)
in a way such that the minimizer
\hat{\delta}
of
D_{n}(\delta)
satisfy
\mathbf{A}_{l}\,\hat{\delta}=r_{n}^{-1}\big(\hat{\bm{\theta}}_{\mathrm{DVCM}}(u_{0})-{\bm{\theta}}(u_{0})\big)
, with
r_{n}=(nh/\gamma)^{-1/2}
. Because
s_{3}\equiv 0
, the Taylor remainder
R_{n}(\delta)
in the generalized DVCM proof (Equation (
C.12
)) vanishes, and consequently we have:

D_{n}(\delta)=-(nh)\,r_{n}\,\delta^{\top}\bar{\Delta}+\frac{1}{2}(nh)\,r_{n}^{2}\,\delta^{\top}\bar{\Lambda}\,\delta,

where

\bar{\Lambda}\coloneqq\frac{1}{nh}\sum_{k=0}^{K}Z_{ki}Z_{ki}^{\top}\,W(t_{k}),\quad\bar{\Delta}\coloneqq\frac{1}{nh}\sum_{k=0}^{K}(Y_{ki}-Z_{ki}^{\top}\bar{\bm{\theta}}(u_{0}))Z_{ki}\,W(t_{k})\,.

Using the fact that the optimal
\delta
satisfies
\nabla D_{n}(\hat{\delta})=0
, we have:

\hat{\delta}=\bar{\Lambda}^{-1}\bar{\Delta}\implies r_{n}^{-1}\big(\hat{\bm{\theta}}_{\mathrm{DVCM}}(u_{0})-{\bm{\theta}}(u_{0})\big)=r_{n}^{-1}\,\mathbf{A}_{l}\,\bar{\Lambda}^{-1}\bar{\Delta}.

(C.22)

where the RHS follows from the definition of
\delta
.
The asymptotic limits of
\mathbb{E}[\Delta_{k}\mid U_{k}=u_{k}]
,
{\sf Var}[\Delta_{k}\mid U_{k}=u_{k}]
, and
\mathbb{E}[\Lambda_{k}\mid U_{k}=u_{k}]
has been shown in Lemma
A.6
.
Integrating the above quantities w.r.t.
U_{k}
with density
f_{\gamma}(u)=(1/\gamma)f\big((u-u^{*})/\gamma\big)
and applying Equation (
C.10
), the values of
\mathbb{E}[\bar{\Delta}]
,
{\sf Var}(\bar{\Delta})
, and
\mathbb{E}[\bar{\Lambda}]
become:

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}[\bar{\Delta}]=O(\gamma^{-1}h^{\beta}),

\displaystyle{\sf Var}(\bar{\Delta})=(\gamma nh)^{-1}\,\sigma^{2}(u_{0})\,\zeta_{0,2}\otimes\Psi(u_{0})\,(1+o(1)),

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\bar{\Lambda}=\gamma^{-1}\zeta_{0,1}\otimes\Psi(u_{0})\,(1+o(1))\,.

In particular, the change–of–variables and integration steps are worked out in detail in Appendix
C.7
for the generalized DVCM case, and the same calculations apply here exactly.
Note that the quantities
\zeta_{r,s}
does not depend on the loss function and consequently remain fixed. Since
\Delta_{k}
are sums of
\varepsilon_{ki}Z_{ki}W(t_{k})
with mean zero conditional on
(X_{ki},U_{k})
,
the Lyapunov (or Lindeberg–Feller) CLT applies, yielding

\sqrt{\gamma nh}\big(\bar{\Delta}-\mathbb{E}\bar{\Delta}\big)\ \xrightarrow{d}\ \mathcal{N}\big(0,\ \zeta_{0,2}\otimes\Psi(u_{0})\,\sigma^{2}(u_{0})\big).

Now we have established the asymptotic normality of
\bar{\Delta}
. Going back to Equation (
C.22
), it can be easily verified that its linear transformation
r_{n}^{-1}\,\mathbf{A}_{l}\,\bar{\Lambda}^{-1}\bar{\Delta}
is such that

r_{n}^{-1}\left(\hat{\bm{\theta}}_{\mathrm{DVCM}}(u_{0})-{\bm{\theta}}(u_{0})-\mathbf{b}_{\mathrm{DVCM}}(u_{0})\right)\xrightarrow{d}\mathcal{N}\left(0,\Omega_{\mathrm{DVCM}}(u_{0})\right)

where

\displaystyle\mathbf{b}_{\mathrm{DVCM}}(u_{0})=O(h^{\beta}),\qquad\Omega_{\mathrm{DVCM}}(u_{0})=\sigma^{2}(u_{0})\,\mathbf{e}_{1,l+1}^{\top}\,\zeta_{0,1}^{-1}\zeta_{0,2}\zeta_{0,1}^{-1}\,\mathbf{e}_{1,l+1}\ \otimes\ \Psi(u_{0})^{-1}\,.

This completes the proof.

C.9

Proof of Lemma
A.10

Recall the rates of
\hat{\bm{\theta}}_{\mathrm{GLR}}(u_{0})
,
\hat{\bm{\theta}}_{\mathrm{GDVCM}}(u_{0})
, and their ratio

\hat{\bm{\theta}}_{\mathrm{GLR}}(u_{0})-{\bm{\theta}}(u_{0})=O_{p}(r_{\mathrm{GLR}}),\quad\hat{\bm{\theta}}_{\mathrm{GDVCM}}(u_{0})-{\bm{\theta}}(u_{0})=O_{p}(r_{\mathrm{GDVCM}}),\qquad\rho\coloneqq\frac{r_{\mathrm{GLR}}}{r_{\mathrm{GDVCM}}},

and assume
Q\asymp_{p}\rho^{2}I
, i.e.
c\rho^{2}\leq\lambda_{\min}(Q)\leq\lambda_{\max}(Q)\leq C\rho^{2}

w.p.
\to 1
. Set
r_{\mathrm{TL}}\coloneqq r_{\mathrm{GLR}}\wedge r_{\mathrm{GDVCM}}
.
Define

D_{n}(\delta)\coloneqq L_{n}({\bm{\theta}}(u_{0})+r_{\mathrm{TL}}\delta)-L_{n}({\bm{\theta}}(u_{0}))\,,

and

L_{n}(\alpha)\coloneqq\frac{1}{n_{0}}\sum_{i\in\mathcal{I}_{0}^{*}}\ell(X_{0i}^{\top}\alpha,Y_{0i})+\frac{1}{2}\|\alpha-\hat{\bm{\theta}}_{\mathrm{GDVCM}}(u_{0})\|_{Q}^{2}\,.

In this proof, we use
\|\cdot\|
to denote
\ell_{2}
-norm of a vector and spectral norm of a matrix.

Part 1: Rate and consistency of TL.

The key step is to show that for any
\varepsilon>0
there exists a large constant
M=M({\epsilon})
such that

{\mathbb{P}}\left\{\ \inf_{\|\delta\|=M}D_{n}(\delta)>0\ \right\}={\mathbb{P}}\left\{\ \inf_{\|\delta\|=M}L_{n}({\bm{\theta}}(u_{0})+r_{\mathrm{TL}}\delta)>L_{n}({\bm{\theta}}(u_{0}))\ \right\}\geq\ 1-\varepsilon.

(C.23)

Since
D_{n}(0)=0
and
D_{n}(\cdot)
is convex, this implies that with probability at
least
1-\varepsilon
a global minimizer lies inside the ball
\{{\bm{\theta}}(u_{0})+r_{\mathrm{TL}}\delta:\ \|\delta\|\leq M\}
. Consequently,

\|\hat{\bm{\theta}}_{\mathrm{TL}}(u_{0})-{\bm{\theta}}(u_{0})\|=O_{p}(r_{\mathrm{TL}}).

Expanding
D_{n}(\delta)
.
A Taylor expansion of
L_{n}({\bm{\theta}}(u_{0})+r_{\mathrm{TL}}\delta)
around
{\bm{\theta}}(u_{0})
gives

D_{n}(\delta)=r_{\mathrm{TL}}\,\delta^{\top}G_{n}+\frac{1}{2}r_{\mathrm{TL}}^{2}\,\delta^{\top}H_{n}\delta+R_{n}(\delta),

(C.24)

with

\displaystyle G_{n}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{n_{0}}\sum_{i\in\mathcal{I}_{0}^{*}}s_{1}(X_{0i}^{\top}{\bm{\theta}}(u_{0}),Y_{0i})X_{0i}+Q({\bm{\theta}}(u_{0})-\hat{\bm{\theta}}_{\mathrm{GDVCM}}(u_{0}))\,,

\displaystyle H_{n}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{n_{0}}\sum_{i\in\mathcal{I}_{0}^{*}}s_{2}(X_{0i}^{\top}{\bm{\theta}}(u_{0}),Y_{0i})X_{0i}X_{0i}^{\top}+Q\,,

\displaystyle R_{n}(\delta)

\displaystyle=\frac{r_{\mathrm{TL}}^{3}}{6n_{0}}\sum_{i\in\mathcal{I}_{0}^{*}}s_{3}(\tilde{\eta}_{0i},Y_{0i})\,(X_{0i}^{\top}\delta)^{3}\,,

with
\tilde{\eta}_{0i}
being some intermediate point between
X_{0i}^{\top}{\bm{\theta}}(u_{0})
and
X_{0i}^{\top}({\bm{\theta}}(u_{0})+r_{\mathrm{TL}}\delta)
.

Order of
G_{n}
,
H_{n}
, and
R_{n}
.
It is immediate from the definition of the remainder term
R_{n}
that for any
M>0
:

\sup_{\|\delta\|\leq M}|R_{n}(\delta)|\leq\frac{r_{\mathrm{TL}}^{3}M^{3}}{6n_{0}}\sum_{i\in\mathcal{I}_{0}^{*}}\big|s_{3}(\tilde{\eta}_{0i},Y_{0i})\big|\,\|X_{0i}\|^{3}=O_{p}(r_{\mathrm{TL}}^{3}M^{3}),

(C.25)

as
\mathbb{E}|s_{3}(\cdot,Y)|^{4}<\infty
and
\|X\|<\infty
by Assumption
2′′
. Our next goal is to obtain the order of
G_{n}
and
H_{n}
. Note that
G_{n}
involves the term
Q({\bm{\theta}}(u_{0})-\hat{\bm{\theta}}_{\mathrm{GDVCM}}(u_{0}))
. From our definition of
r_{\mathrm{GDVCM}}
, we have:

{\bm{\theta}}(u_{0})-\hat{\bm{\theta}}_{\mathrm{GDVCM}}(u_{0})=O_{p}(r_{\mathrm{GDVCM}})\,,

and by our assumption on
Q
, we have
Q\asymp_{p}\rho^{2}I
. Furthermore, from the standard property of the (centered) score function on the target domain, we have:

\frac{1}{n_{0}}\sum_{i\in\mathcal{I}_{0}^{*}}s_{1}(X_{0i}^{\top}{\bm{\theta}}(u_{0}),Y_{0i})X_{0i}=O_{p}(n_{0}^{-1/2})=O_{p}(r_{\mathrm{GLR}})\,.

Therefore, combining these orders, we have:

G_{n}=O_{p}\big(r_{\mathrm{GLR}}+\rho^{2}r_{\mathrm{GDVCM}}\big).

(C.26)

Furthermore, we have
\lambda_{\min}(\Psi(u_{0}))\geq c_{0}^{\prime}
by Assumption
2′′
. Therefore, by the law of large numbers:

\displaystyle\lambda_{\min}(H_{n})

\displaystyle\geq\lambda_{\min}(\Psi(u_{0})+Q)+o_{p}(1)

\displaystyle\geq\lambda_{\min}(\Psi(u_{0}))+\lambda_{\min}(Q)+o_{p}(1)

\displaystyle=O_{p}(1+\rho^{2})

(C.27)

Equivalent characterizations of
r_{\mathrm{TL}}
.

Observe that

\frac{r_{\mathrm{GLR}}+\rho^{2}r_{\mathrm{GDVCM}}}{1+\rho^{2}}=\begin{cases}r_{\mathrm{GLR}}\dfrac{1+\rho}{1+\rho^{2}}\leq 2\,r_{\mathrm{GLR}},&\rho\leq 1,\\
r_{\mathrm{GDVCM}}\dfrac{\rho+\rho^{2}}{1+\rho^{2}}\leq 2\,r_{\mathrm{GDVCM}},&\rho\geq 1,\end{cases}

and thus we obtain lower bound on
r_{\mathrm{TL}}

\frac{r_{\mathrm{GLR}}+\rho^{2}r_{\mathrm{GDVCM}}}{1+\rho^{2}}\leq 2(r_{\mathrm{GLR}}\wedge r_{\mathrm{GDVCM}})\coloneqq 2r_{\mathrm{TL}}\ \implies\ r_{\mathrm{TL}}\geq\frac{r_{\mathrm{GLR}}+\rho^{2}r_{\mathrm{GDVCM}}}{2(1+\rho^{2})}\,.

(C.28)

Furthermore, we also have:

r_{\mathrm{TL}}=\min\{r_{\mathrm{GLR}},r_{\mathrm{GDVCM}}\}\leq\frac{r_{\mathrm{GLR}}+\rho^{2}\,r_{\mathrm{GDVCM}}}{1+\rho^{2}}.

Combining this with (
C.28
) yields the bound

\frac{r_{\mathrm{GLR}}+\rho^{2}r_{\mathrm{GDVCM}}}{2(1+\rho^{2})}\ \leq\ r_{\mathrm{TL}}\ \leq\ \frac{r_{\mathrm{GLR}}+\rho^{2}r_{\mathrm{GDVCM}}}{1+\rho^{2}}\ \implies\ r_{\mathrm{TL}}\asymp\frac{r_{\mathrm{GLR}}+\rho^{2}r_{\mathrm{GDVCM}}}{1+\rho^{2}}\,.

(C.29)

Verifying Objective (
C.23
).
Recall the definition of
D_{n}(\delta)
from Equation (
C.24
). We have on the sphere
\{\|\delta\|=M\}
:

\begin{split}\inf_{\|\delta\|=M}D_{n}(\delta)&\geq-\underbrace{r_{\mathrm{TL}}\|G_{n}\|}_{\coloneqq A_{n}\geq 0}\,M+\underbrace{\frac{1}{2}r_{\mathrm{TL}}^{2}\lambda_{\min}(H_{n})}_{\coloneqq B_{n}\geq 0}M^{2}-\sup_{\|\delta\|\leq M}|R_{n}(\delta)|\\
&=-A_{n}M+B_{n}M^{2}-\sup_{\|\delta\|\leq M}|R_{n}(\delta)|\,.\end{split}

(C.30)

The order of
A_{n}
and
B_{n}
can be derived from the order of
G_{n}
and
\lambda_{\min}(H_{n})
respectively. In particular, using the order of
G_{n}
, as established in Equation (
C.26
), we have:

A_{n}=r_{\mathrm{TL}}\|G_{n}\|=O_{p}\left(r_{\mathrm{TL}}\left(r_{\mathrm{GLR}}+\rho^{2}r_{\mathrm{GDVCM}}\right)\right).

Moreove, the lower bound on the minimum eigenvalue of
H_{n}
(Equation (
C.9
)) and equation on
r_{\mathrm{TL}}
in Equation (
C.29
) yield the following order of
B_{n}
:

B_{n}=\frac{1}{2}r_{\mathrm{TL}}^{2}\lambda_{\min}(H_{n})=\frac{1}{2}r_{\mathrm{TL}}\cdot\left(\frac{r_{\mathrm{GLR}}+\rho^{2}r_{\mathrm{GDVCM}}}{2(1+\rho^{2})}\right)\,\Omega_{p}(1+\rho^{2})=\Omega_{p}\left(r_{\mathrm{TL}}\left(r_{\mathrm{GLR}}+\rho^{2}r_{\mathrm{GDVCM}}\right)\right)\,.

Treating
M
as a constant, the upper bound on the remainder term (Equation (
C.25
)) yields

\sup_{\|\delta\|\leq M}|R_{n}(\delta)|=O_{p}\Big(r_{\mathrm{TL}}^{3}\Big)\,,

and by Equation (
C.29
), as well as the immediate relation
1+\rho^{2}\gtrsim 1
,

O_{p}(r_{\mathrm{TL}}^{3})=o_{p}(r_{\mathrm{TL}}^{2})=o_{p}\left(r_{\mathrm{TL}}\frac{r_{\mathrm{GLR}}+\rho^{2}r_{\mathrm{GDVCM}}}{1+\rho^{2}}\right)=o_{p}\left(r_{\mathrm{TL}}(r_{\mathrm{GLR}}+\rho^{2}r_{\mathrm{GDVCM}})\right)\,.

Hence the remainder
\sup_{\|\delta\|\leq M}|R_{n}(\delta)|
is dominated by both
A_{n}
and
B_{n}
, so Equation (
C.30
) is written as

\inf_{\|\delta\|=M}D_{n}(\delta)\geq-A_{n}M+B_{n}M^{2}+o\left(A_{n}\wedge B_{n}\right)\,.

Both
A_{n}
and
B_{n}
are of order
O_{p}\left(r_{\mathrm{TL}}\left(r_{\mathrm{GLR}}+\rho^{2}r_{\mathrm{GDVCM}}\right)\right)
so by choosing large enough
M
,
B_{n}M^{2}
dominates
-A_{n}M
uniformly in
M
. Therefore we have verified that by setting
M
to be large enough, the condition in (
C.23
) is verified, and this further implies

\big\|\hat{\bm{\theta}}_{\mathrm{TL}}(u_{0})-{\bm{\theta}}(u_{0})\big\|=O_{p}(r_{\mathrm{TL}})\,.

Part 2: Asymptotic distribution.

Let
s_{j}(\eta,y)=\partial^{j}\ell(\eta,y)/\partial\eta^{j}
,

{\bm{\tau}}_{\mathrm{TL}}\coloneqq\hat{\bm{\theta}}_{\mathrm{TL}}(u_{0})-{\bm{\theta}}(u_{0})
and

{\bm{\tau}}_{\mathrm{GDVCM}}\coloneqq\hat{\bm{\theta}}_{\mathrm{GDVCM}}(u_{0})-{\bm{\theta}}(u_{0})
.
The first–order condition is

\frac{1}{n_{0}}\sum_{i\in\mathcal{I}_{0}^{*}}s_{1}\big(X_{0i}^{\top}\hat{\bm{\theta}}_{\mathrm{TL}}(u_{0}),Y_{0i}\big)X_{0i}+Q\big(\hat{\bm{\theta}}_{\mathrm{TL}}(u_{0})-\hat{\bm{\theta}}_{\mathrm{GDVCM}}(u_{0})\big)=0.

(C.31)

A second–order Taylor expansion of
s_{1}
at first argument around
{\bm{\theta}}(u_{0})
gives

s_{1}\big(X_{0i}^{\top}\hat{\bm{\theta}}_{\mathrm{TL}}(u_{0}),Y_{0i}\big)=s_{1}\big(X_{0i}^{\top}{\bm{\theta}}(u_{0}),Y_{0i}\big)+s_{2}\big(X_{0i}^{\top}{\bm{\theta}}(u_{0}),Y_{0i}\big)\,X_{0i}^{\top}{\bm{\tau}}_{\mathrm{TL}}+\frac{1}{2}\,s_{3}(\bar{\eta}_{0i},Y_{0i})\big(X_{0i}^{\top}{\bm{\tau}}_{\mathrm{TL}}\big)^{2},

where
\bar{\eta}_{0i}
lies between
X_{0i}^{\top}{\bm{\theta}}(u_{0})
and
X_{0i}^{\top}\hat{\bm{\theta}}_{\mathrm{TL}}(u_{0})
.
Plug into (
C.31
) and collect terms to obtain

\frac{1}{n_{0}}\mathbf{X}_{0}^{\top}S_{1}+\Big(\frac{1}{n_{0}}\mathbf{X}_{0}^{\top}S_{2}\mathbf{X}_{0}+Q\Big){\bm{\tau}}_{\mathrm{TL}}+\frac{1}{2n_{0}}\sum_{i\in\mathcal{I}_{0}^{*}}s_{3}(\bar{\eta}_{0i},Y_{0i})\,X_{0i}\big(X_{0i}^{\top}{\bm{\tau}}_{\mathrm{TL}}\big)^{2}=Q{\bm{\tau}}_{\mathrm{GDVCM}},

(C.32)

where
\mathbf{X}_{0}\in{\mathbb{R}}^{n_{0}\times p}
with
X_{0i}
being its
i^{th}
row,
S_{1}\in{\mathbb{R}}^{n_{0}}
with entries being
s_{1}(X_{0i}^{\top}{\bm{\theta}}(u_{0}),Y_{0i})
, and
S_{2}\in{\mathbb{R}}^{n_{0}\times n_{0}}
is a diagonal matrix with entries
s_{2}(X_{0i}^{\top}{\bm{\theta}}(u_{0}),Y_{0i})
. By
\mathbb{E}|s_{3}(\cdot,Y)|^{4}<\infty
and
\|X\|<\infty

\frac{1}{n_{0}}\sum_{i\in\mathcal{I}_{0}^{*}}\big|s_{3}(\bar{\eta}_{0i},Y_{0i})\big|\,\|X_{0i}\|^{3}=O_{p}(1),

and thus

\Big\|\frac{1}{2n_{0}}\sum_{i}s_{3}(\bar{\eta}_{0i},Y_{0i})\,X_{0i}\big(X_{0i}^{\top}{\bm{\tau}}_{\mathrm{TL}}\big)^{2}\Big\|\leq\frac{1}{n_{0}}\sum_{i}\big|s_{3}(\bar{\eta}_{0i},Y_{0i})\big|\,\|X_{0i}\|^{3}\,\|{\bm{\tau}}_{\mathrm{TL}}\|^{2}=O_{p}\big(\|{\bm{\tau}}_{\mathrm{TL}}\|^{2}\big).

(C.33)

By the law of large numbers and Assumption
2′′
,

\frac{1}{n_{0}}\mathbf{X}_{0}^{\top}S_{2}\mathbf{X}_{0}=\Psi(u_{0})+o_{p}(1).

(C.34)

Recall
{\bm{\tau}}_{\mathrm{GLR}}\coloneqq\hat{\bm{\theta}}_{\mathrm{GLR}}(u_{0})-{\bm{\theta}}(u_{0})
. The score equation of generalized linear model gives

0=\frac{1}{n_{0}}\sum_{i\in\mathcal{I}_{0}^{*}}s_{1}(X_{0i}^{\top}\hat{\bm{\theta}}_{\mathrm{GLR}},Y_{0i})X_{0i}=\frac{1}{n_{0}}\mathbf{X}_{0}^{\top}S_{1}+\Big(\frac{1}{n_{0}}\mathbf{X}_{0}^{\top}S_{2}\mathbf{X}_{0}\Big){\bm{\tau}}_{\mathrm{GLR}}+R_{n_{0}}.

Reorganizing terms yields

-\frac{1}{n_{0}}\mathbf{X}_{0}^{\top}S_{1}=\Big(\frac{1}{n_{0}}\mathbf{X}_{0}^{\top}S_{2}\mathbf{X}_{0}\Big){\bm{\tau}}_{\mathrm{GLR}}+R_{n_{0}}.

By standard theory of generalized linear model, it follows that
\frac{1}{n_{0}}\mathbf{X}_{0}^{\top}S_{2}\mathbf{X}_{0}=\Psi(u_{0})+o_{p}(1)
and
\|R_{n_{0}}\|=o_{p}(r_{\mathrm{GLR}})
. Therefore,

-\frac{1}{n_{0}}\mathbf{X}_{0}^{\top}S_{1}=\Psi(u_{0}){\bm{\tau}}_{\mathrm{GLR}}+o_{p}(r_{\mathrm{GLR}}).

(C.35)

Rearranging (
C.32
) yields

r_{\mathrm{TL}}^{-1}{\bm{\tau}}_{\mathrm{TL}}=r_{\mathrm{TL}}^{-1}\Big(\frac{1}{n_{0}}\mathbf{X}_{0}^{\top}S_{2}\mathbf{X}_{0}+Q\Big)^{-1}\Big(Q{\bm{\tau}}_{\mathrm{GDVCM}}-\frac{1}{n_{0}}\mathbf{X}_{0}^{\top}S_{1}\Big)+\tilde{R}_{n}

with

\tilde{R}_{n}=-r_{\mathrm{TL}}^{-1}\Big(\frac{1}{n_{0}}\mathbf{X}_{0}^{\top}S_{2}\mathbf{X}_{0}+Q\Big)^{-1}\frac{1}{2n_{0}}\sum_{i\in\mathcal{I}_{0}^{*}}s_{3}(\bar{\eta}_{0i},Y_{0i})\,X_{0i}\big(X_{0i}^{\top}{\bm{\tau}}_{\mathrm{TL}}\big)^{2}\,.

Substituting the Equations (
C.33
) and (
C.34
), it follows from the consistency of
\hat{\bm{\theta}}_{\mathrm{TL}}(u_{0})
that

\tilde{R}_{n}=r_{\mathrm{TL}}^{-1}\Big(\Psi(u_{0})+Q\Big)^{-1}O_{p}(\|{\bm{\tau}}_{\mathrm{TL}}\|^{2})=O_{p}(\|{\bm{\tau}}_{\mathrm{TL}}\|)=o_{p}(1)\,,

and thus

r_{\mathrm{TL}}^{-1}{\bm{\tau}}_{\mathrm{TL}}=r_{\mathrm{TL}}^{-1}\Big(\frac{1}{n_{0}}\mathbf{X}_{0}^{\top}S_{2}\mathbf{X}_{0}+Q\Big)^{-1}\Big(Q{\bm{\tau}}_{\mathrm{GDVCM}}-\frac{1}{n_{0}}\mathbf{X}_{0}^{\top}S_{1}\Big)+o_{p}(1)\,.

Now we substitute the Equations (
C.34
) and (
C.35
), and obtain

r_{\mathrm{TL}}^{-1}{\bm{\tau}}_{\mathrm{TL}}=r_{\mathrm{TL}}^{-1}\Big(\Psi(u_{0})+Q\Big)^{-1}\Big\{\,Q\,{\bm{\tau}}_{\mathrm{GDVCM}}+\Psi(u_{0}){\bm{\tau}}_{\mathrm{GLR}}\Big\}+o_{p}(1).

(C.36)

By sample splitting,
{\bm{\tau}}_{\mathrm{GLR}}
is independent of
{\bm{\tau}}_{\mathrm{GDVCM}}
, based on which we will derive the regime–specific asymptotic normality.

Regime
\rho\to 0
(GLR–dominated).

From
Q=O_{p}(\rho^{2})
, we have
(\Psi(u_{0})+Q)^{-1}=\Psi^{-1}(u_{0})+o_{p}(1)
and

\|\Psi^{-1}(u_{0})Q{\bm{\tau}}_{\mathrm{GDVCM}}\|=O_{p}(\rho^{2}\,\|{\bm{\tau}}_{\mathrm{GDVCM}}\|)=O_{p}(\rho^{2}r_{\mathrm{GDVCM}})=O_{p}(r_{\mathrm{GLR}}\,\rho)=o_{p}(r_{\mathrm{GLR}}).

Hence
{\bm{\tau}}_{\mathrm{TL}}={\bm{\tau}}_{\mathrm{GLR}}+o_{p}(r_{\mathrm{GLR}})
. By Slutsky’s theorem and part 1 of Proposition
A.9

r_{\mathrm{GLR}}^{-1}\big(\hat{\bm{\theta}}_{\mathrm{TL}}(u_{0})-{\bm{\theta}}(u_{0})\big)\ \xrightarrow{d}\ \mathcal{N}\big(0,\ \Omega_{\mathrm{GLR}}(u_{0})\big).

Regime
\rho\to\infty
(DVCM–dominated).

Starting from

{\bm{\tau}}_{\mathrm{TL}}=(\Psi(u_{0})+Q)^{-1}\left\{\,Q\,{\bm{\tau}}_{\mathrm{GDVCM}}+\Psi(u_{0})\,{\bm{\tau}}_{\mathrm{GLR}}\right\}+o_{p}(r_{\mathrm{TL}}),

use the identity

(\Psi(u_{0})+Q)^{-1}-Q^{-1}=-(\Psi(u_{0})+Q)^{-1}\,\Psi(u_{0})\,Q^{-1}.

Since
\lambda_{\min}(Q)\asymp_{p}\rho^{2}
and
\Psi(u_{0})\succeq c_{0}^{\prime}I
,

\|(\Psi(u_{0})+Q)^{-1}\|\leq\|Q^{-1}\|=O_{p}(\rho^{-2}),\qquad\|\Psi(u_{0})\|=O(1),

hence

\big\|(\Psi(u_{0})+Q)^{-1}-Q^{-1}\big\|\leq\|(\Psi(u_{0})+Q)^{-1}\|\,\|\Psi(u_{0})\|\,\|Q^{-1}\|=O_{p}(\rho^{-4})=o_{p}\big(\|Q^{-1}\|\big).

(C.37)

Next, apply the identity
(\Psi(u_{0})+Q)^{-1}Q=I-(\Psi(u_{0})+Q)^{-1}\Psi(u_{0})
to get

{\bm{\tau}}_{\mathrm{TL}}={\bm{\tau}}_{\mathrm{GDVCM}}-(\Psi(u_{0})+Q)^{-1}\Psi(u_{0})\,{\bm{\tau}}_{\mathrm{GDVCM}}+(\Psi(u_{0})+Q)^{-1}\Psi(u_{0})\,{\bm{\tau}}_{\mathrm{GLR}}+o_{p}(r_{\mathrm{GDVCM}}).

The second and third terms are negligible by Equation (
C.37
):

\big\|(\Psi(u_{0})+Q)^{-1}\Psi(u_{0})\,{\bm{\tau}}_{\mathrm{GDVCM}}\big\|\leq\|Q^{-1}\|\,\|\Psi(u_{0})\|\,\|{\bm{\tau}}_{\mathrm{GDVCM}}\|\{1+o_{p}(1)\}=O_{p}(\rho^{-2}r_{\mathrm{GDVCM}})=o_{p}(r_{\mathrm{GDVCM}}),

and, using
r_{\mathrm{GLR}}=\rho\,r_{\mathrm{GDVCM}}
and Equation (
C.37
),

\big\|(\Psi(u_{0})+Q)^{-1}\Psi(u_{0})\,{\bm{\tau}}_{\mathrm{GLR}}\big\|\leq\|Q^{-1}\|\,\|\Psi(u_{0})\|\,\|{\bm{\tau}}_{\mathrm{GLR}}\|\{1+o_{p}(1)\}=O_{p}(\rho^{-2}r_{\mathrm{GLR}})=O_{p}(r_{\mathrm{GDVCM}}/\rho)=o_{p}(r_{\mathrm{GDVCM}}).

Therefore,

{\bm{\tau}}_{\mathrm{TL}}={\bm{\tau}}_{\mathrm{GDVCM}}+o_{p}(r_{\mathrm{GDVCM}}),

and by Slutsky’s theorem and part 2 of Proposition
A.9

r_{\mathrm{GDVCM}}^{-1}\Big(\hat{\bm{\theta}}_{\mathrm{TL}}(u_{0})-{\bm{\theta}}(u_{0})-\mathbf{b}_{\mathrm{GDVCM}}(u_{0})\Big)\ \xrightarrow{d}\ \mathcal{N}\big(0,\ \Omega_{\mathrm{GDVCM}}(u_{0})\big).

Appendix D

Discussion of Assumption
3.2

In this section, we examine the plausibility and technical strength of Assumption
3.2
, which requires boundedness of
\|\mathbf{A}_{l}(\mathbf{Z}^{\top}\mathbf{W}\mathbf{Z})^{-1}\mathbf{A}_{l}^{\top}\|_{2}
. Our analysis considers two asymptotic regimes based on the number of tasks
K
:
(i) a fixed-
K
regime with
n_{0}\to\infty
, and
(ii) a growing-task regime with
K\to\infty
.

The corresponding results are established in Proposition
D.1
and Proposition
D.3
, respectively. In both regimes, we show that Assumption
3.2
is satisfied in the sense of almost sure convergence under mild conditions, thereby providing theoretical justification for its use in our framework.

Proposition D.1

Assume that

\lambda_{\min}\!\big(\mathbb{E}[X_{0i}X_{0i}^{\top}]\big)\ \geq\ \kappa,\qquad\frac{n_{0}}{\sum_{k=0}^{K}n_{k}}\ \geq\ c_{0}

for some constants
\kappa,c_{0}>0
. Then it holds that

{\mathbb{P}}\left(\limsup_{n_{0}\to\infty}\big\|\mathbf{A}_{l}(\mathbf{Z}^{\top}\mathbf{W}\mathbf{Z})^{-1}\mathbf{A}_{l}^{\top}\big\|_{2}\leq C\right)=1

for some constant
C>0
.

Proof D.2

Recall
