\mathbf{Z}^{\top}\mathbf{W}\mathbf{Z}=w_{0}\sum_{i\in\mathcal{I}_{0}}\mathbf{Z}_{0i}\mathbf{Z}_{0i}^{\top}+\sum_{k=1}^{K}w_{k}\sum_{i\in\mathcal{I}_{k}}\mathbf{Z}_{ki}\mathbf{Z}_{ki}^{\top},\qquad w_{k}=\frac{W\!\big((U_{k}-u_{0})/h\big)}{S_{h}},\ S_{h}=\sum_{k=0}^{K}n_{k}W\!\Big(\frac{U_{k}-u_{0}}{h}\Big).

Using
\mathbf{Z}_{0i}=\mathbf{A}_{l}^{\top}X_{0i}
, define

B:=w_{0}\sum_{i\in\mathcal{I}_{0}}X_{0i}X_{0i}^{\top},\qquad M:=\sum_{k=1}^{K}w_{k}\sum_{i\in\mathcal{I}_{k}}\mathbf{Z}_{ki}\mathbf{Z}_{ki}^{\top},

so that
\mathbf{Z}^{\top}\mathbf{W}\mathbf{Z}=\mathbf{A}_{l}^{\top}B\mathbf{A}_{l}+M
.

Let
T:=\mathbf{A}_{l}M^{-1}\mathbf{A}_{l}^{\top}\succeq 0
. The Woodbury identity yields

\mathbf{A}_{l}(\mathbf{Z}^{\top}\mathbf{W}\mathbf{Z})^{-1}\mathbf{A}_{l}^{\top}=T-T(B^{-1}+T)^{-1}T\preceq B^{-1},

hence

\big\|\mathbf{A}_{l}(\mathbf{Z}^{\top}\mathbf{W}\mathbf{Z})^{-1}\mathbf{A}_{l}^{\top}\big\|_{2}\leq\|B^{-1}\|_{2}=\lambda_{\min}(B)^{-1}.

(D.1)

Since
w_{0}=W(0)/S_{h}
and
S_{h}\leq W(0)\sum_{k=0}^{K}n_{k}
, we have

w_{0}=\frac{W(0)}{S_{h}}\ \geq\ \frac{1}{\sum_{k=0}^{K}n_{k}}.

Therefore,

\lambda_{\min}(B)=w_{0}\,\lambda_{\min}\!\left(\sum_{i\in\mathcal{I}_{0}}X_{0i}X_{0i}^{\top}\right)\geq\frac{1}{\sum_{k=0}^{K}n_{k}}\,\lambda_{\min}\!\left(\sum_{i\in\mathcal{I}_{0}}X_{0i}X_{0i}^{\top}\right).

Using
\sum_{k=0}^{K}n_{k}\leq n_{0}/c_{0}
, this becomes

\lambda_{\min}(B)\geq c_{0}\,\lambda_{\min}\!\left(\frac{1}{n_{0}}\sum_{i\in\mathcal{I}_{0}}X_{0i}X_{0i}^{\top}\right).

(D.2)

Let
\Sigma_{0}:=\mathbb{E}[X_{0i}X_{0i}^{\top}]
with
\lambda_{\min}(\Sigma_{0})\geq\kappa
.
Since
X_{0i}X_{0i}^{\top}
are i.i.d. PSD matrices with

\lambda_{\max}(X_{0i}X_{0i}^{\top})=\|X_{0i}\|_{2}^{2}\leq 1
,
the matrix Chernoff bound
[
43
]
gives that for any
\eta\in(0,1)
,

{\mathbb{P}}\!\left\{\lambda_{\min}\!\left(\sum_{i\in\mathcal{I}_{0}}X_{0i}X_{0i}^{\top}\right)\leq(1-\eta)\,n_{0}\,\kappa\right\}\leq p{\sf exp}\!\left(-\frac{\eta^{2}\,n_{0}\,\kappa}{2}\right).

Taking
\eta=1/2
yields

{\mathbb{P}}\!\left\{\lambda_{\min}\!\left(\frac{1}{n_{0}}\sum_{i\in\mathcal{I}_{0}}X_{0i}X_{0i}^{\top}\right)\leq\frac{\kappa}{2}\right\}\leq p{\sf exp}\!\left(-c\,n_{0}\,\kappa\right)

(D.3)

for a constant
c>0
.

On the complement of the event in (
D.3
), we have

\lambda_{\min}(\frac{1}{n_{0}}\sum X_{0i}X_{0i}^{\top})\geq\kappa/2
and thus by
(
D.2
),

\lambda_{\min}(B)\geq c_{0}\cdot\frac{\kappa}{2}.

Plugging into (
D.1
) gives

\big\|\mathbf{A}_{l}(\mathbf{Z}^{\top}\mathbf{W}\mathbf{Z})^{-1}\mathbf{A}_{l}^{\top}\big\|_{2}\leq\frac{2}{c_{0}\kappa}=:C.

Therefore, define
B_{n_{0}}=\Big\{\big\|\mathbf{A}_{l}(\mathbf{Z}^{\top}\mathbf{W}\mathbf{Z})^{-1}\mathbf{A}_{l}^{\top}\big\|_{2}>C\Big\}
we have

{\mathbb{P}}(B_{n_{0}})\leq p{\sf exp}(-C^{\prime}n_{0}\kappa),

which implies for some
k_{0}>0

\sum_{n_{0}=k_{0}}^{\infty}{\mathbb{P}}(B_{n_{0}})\leq\sum_{n_{0}=k_{0}}^{\infty}p{\sf exp}(-C^{\prime}n_{0}\kappa)<\infty.

Applying Borel–Cantelli lemma proves the result.

Proposition D.3

Assume that
\lambda_{\min}\!\big(\mathbb{E}[XX^{\top}\mid U=u]\big)\geq\lambda_{0}
for all
u\in\mathcal{U}
and
Kh/\gamma\gtrsim\log(K)
, then it holds

{\mathbb{P}}\left(\limsup_{K\to\infty}\big\|\mathbf{A}_{l}(\mathbf{Z}^{\top}\mathbf{W}\mathbf{Z})^{-1}\mathbf{A}_{l}^{\top}\big\|_{2}\leq C\right)=1

for some constant
C>0
.

Proof D.4

For a fixed
K
, define the quantities

\mathbf{Y}_{k}:=\sum_{i=1}^{n_{k}}\mathbf{Y}_{ki}=\frac{W_{k}}{S_{h}}\Big(\sum_{i=1}^{n_{k}}X_{ki}X_{ki}^{\top}\Big)\otimes(r_{k}r_{k}^{\top}),\qquad\mathbf{S}^{(K)}=\mathbf{Z}^{\top}\mathbf{W}\mathbf{Z}=\sum_{k=1}^{K}\mathbf{Y}_{k},

where
W_{k}:=W((U_{k}-u_{0})/h)
is the uniform kernel indicator,

S_{h}:=\sum_{k=1}^{K}n_{k}W_{k}
, and
r_{k}=\Phi_{l}((U_{k}-u_{0})/h)
.

1. Spectral bound.

Condition on
U_{1:K}
. Then
W_{k},S_{h},r_{k}
are deterministic and
\{\mathbf{Y}_{k}\}_{k=1}^{K}
are conditionally independent.
Moreover, by boundedness of
\|X_{ki}\|_{2}^{2}
in Assumption
3.2
and
\|r_{k}\|_{2}^{2}
on the kernel window,
there exists a constant
R>0
such that

\Big\|\sum_{i=1}^{n_{k}}X_{ki}X_{ki}^{\top}\Big\|_{2}\leq Rn_{k},\qquad\|r_{k}r_{k}^{\top}\|_{2}=\|r_{k}\|_{2}^{2}\leq R.

Therefore, applying
n_{k}/\bar{n}\leq b_{0}
in Assumption
3.2
,

\|\mathbf{Y}_{k}\|_{2}\leq\frac{W_{k}}{S_{h}}\Big\|\sum_{i=1}^{n_{k}}X_{ki}X_{ki}^{\top}\Big\|_{2}\|r_{k}r_{k}^{\top}\|_{2}\leq R^{2}\frac{n_{k}W_{k}}{S_{h}}\leq R^{2}\frac{n_{k}}{\sum_{s=1}^{K}n_{s}}=\frac{R^{2}}{K}\frac{n_{k}}{\bar{n}}\leq\frac{R^{2}b_{0}}{K}.

Define a quantity (to be used later)

R_{0}:=\frac{R^{2}b_{0}}{K}.

(D.4)

2. Conditional matrix Chernoff.

Let
\mathbf{M}(U):=\mathbb{E}[\mathbf{S}^{(K)}\mid U_{1:K}]
and
\mu(U):=\lambda_{\min}(\mathbf{M}(U))
.
Applying the matrix multiplicative Chernoff bound
[
43
]
conditional on
U_{1:K}

yields, for any
\varepsilon\in(0,1)
,

{\mathbb{P}}\!\left(\lambda_{\min}(\mathbf{S}^{(K)})\leq(1-\varepsilon)\mu(U)\ \Big|\ U_{1:K}\right)\leq d_{0}{\sf exp}\!\left(-\frac{\varepsilon^{2}\,\mu(U)}{2R_{0}}\right).

(D.5)

Now we want to lower bound
\mu(U)

Using
\mathbb{E}[XX^{\top}\mid U=u]\succeq\lambda_{0}I_{p}
,

\mathbb{E}\!\left[\sum_{i=1}^{n_{k}}X_{ki}X_{ki}^{\top}\ \Big|\ U_{k}\right]=n_{k}\,\mathbb{E}[XX^{\top}\mid U_{k}]\succeq n_{k}\lambda_{0}I_{p}.

Hence,

\displaystyle\mathbf{M}(U)

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}[\mathbf{S}^{(K)}\mid U_{1:K}]=\sum_{k=1}^{K}\frac{W_{k}}{S_{h}}\,\mathbb{E}\!\left[\sum_{i=1}^{n_{k}}X_{ki}X_{ki}^{\top}\Big|\ U_{k}\right]\otimes(r_{k}r_{k}^{\top})

\displaystyle\succeq\lambda_{0}\,I_{p}\otimes\sum_{k=1}^{K}\frac{n_{k}W_{k}}{S_{h}}\,r_{k}r_{k}^{\top}.

Therefore,

\mu(U)\ \geq\ \lambda_{0}\,\lambda_{\min}\!\left(\sum_{k=1}^{K}\alpha_{k}r_{k}r_{k}^{\top}\right),\qquad\alpha_{k}:=\frac{n_{k}W_{k}}{S_{h}}.

(D.6)

3. Concentration of the moment matrix.

Define the unnormalized
(l+1)\times(l+1)
matrix

\mathbf{G}:=\sum_{k=1}^{K}n_{k}W_{k}\,r_{k}r_{k}^{\top},\qquad\text{so that}\qquad\sum_{k=1}^{K}\alpha_{k}r_{k}r_{k}^{\top}=\frac{\mathbf{G}}{S_{h}}\,.

Hence

\lambda_{\min}\!\left(\sum_{k=1}^{K}\alpha_{k}r_{k}r_{k}^{\top}\right)=\frac{\lambda_{\min}(\mathbf{G})}{S_{h}}.

(D.7)

We first lower bound
\lambda_{\min}(\mathbf{G})
.
Let
\mathbf{T}_{k}:=n_{k}W_{k}r_{k}r_{k}^{\top}\succeq 0
, so
\mathbf{G}=\sum_{k=1}^{K}\mathbf{T}_{k}
.
Because
W_{k}
and
r_{k}
depend only on
U_{k}
, the matrices
\{\mathbf{T}_{k}\}
are independent.
Moreover, on the window
\|r_{k}\|_{2}^{2}\leq R
and
n_{k}\leq b_{0}\bar{n}
, so

\lambda_{\max}(\mathbf{T}_{k})\leq n_{k}\|r_{k}\|_{2}^{2}\leq b_{0}\bar{n}\,R=:R_{A}.

Let
\mathbf{M}_{G}:=\mathbb{E}[\mathbf{G}]=\sum_{k=1}^{K}n_{k}\,\mathbb{E}[W_{k}r_{k}r_{k}^{\top}]
and

\mu_{G}:=\lambda_{\min}(\mathbf{M}_{G})
.
Using the density lower bound on
[u_{0}-h,u_{0}+h]
and the change of variables
t=(u-u_{0})/h
,

\mathbb{E}[W_{k}r_{k}r_{k}^{\top}]=\int_{u_{0}-h}^{u_{0}+h}\Phi_{l}\!\Big(\frac{u-u_{0}}{h}\Big)\Phi_{l}\!\Big(\frac{u-u_{0}}{h}\Big)^{\top}f_{U}(u)\,du\succeq\frac{a_{0}^{\prime}h}{\gamma}\int_{-1}^{1}\Phi_{l}(t)\Phi_{l}(t)^{\top}\,dt.

There exists
\kappa_{0}
such that
\int_{-1}^{1}\Phi_{l}(t)\Phi_{l}(t)^{\top}\,dt\geq\kappa_{0}>0
.
Then

\mu_{G}\ \geq\ \frac{a_{0}^{\prime}h}{\gamma}\,\kappa_{0}\sum_{k=1}^{K}n_{k}=\frac{a_{0}^{\prime}h}{\gamma}\,\kappa_{0}\,K\bar{n}.

(D.8)

Applying matrix Chernoff to
\mathbf{G}=\sum_{k=1}^{K}\mathbf{T}_{k}
yields, for any
\eta\in(0,1)
,

{\mathbb{P}}\!\left\{\lambda_{\min}(\mathbf{G})\leq(1-\eta)\mu_{G}\right\}\leq(l+1){\sf exp}\!\left(-\frac{\eta^{2}\mu_{G}}{2R_{A}}\right)\leq(l+1){\sf exp}\!\left(-c_{1}\,\frac{Kh}{\gamma}\right),

(D.9)

for some constant
c_{1}>0
.

4. Upper bound
S_{h}

We also control
S_{h}=\sum_{k=1}^{K}n_{k}W_{k}
from above. Note that
0\leq n_{k}W_{k}\leq n_{k}\leq b_{0}\bar{n}

and
\{W_{k}\}
are independent. Moreover, by the density upper bound in Assumption
3.2
,

\mathbb{E}[W_{k}]={\mathbb{P}}(|U_{k}-u_{0}|\leq h)=\int_{u_{0}-h}^{u_{0}+h}f_{U}(u)\,du\ \leq\ \frac{2a_{0}h}{\gamma}.

Thus

\mathbb{E}[S_{h}]=\sum_{k=1}^{K}n_{k}\mathbb{E}[W_{k}]\ \leq\ \frac{2a_{0}h}{\gamma}\sum_{k=1}^{K}n_{k}=\frac{2a_{0}h}{\gamma}\,K\bar{n}.

A Chernoff bound gives that for any
t\in(0,1)
,

{\mathbb{P}}\!\left\{S_{h}\geq(1+t)\mathbb{E}[S_{h}]\right\}\leq{\sf exp}\!\left(-c_{2}\,\frac{Kh}{\gamma}\right)

(D.10)

for some constant
c_{2}>0
.

5. probability on “good event”.

Let

\mathcal{E}:=\Big\{\lambda_{\min}(\mathbf{G})\geq(1-\eta)\mu_{G}\Big\}\cap\Big\{S_{h}\leq(1+t)\mathbb{E}[S_{h}]\Big\}.

On
\mathcal{E}
, using (
D.7
), (
D.8
), and
\mathbb{E}[S_{h}]\leq(2a_{0}h/\gamma)K\bar{n}
,

\lambda_{\min}\!\left(\sum_{k=1}^{K}\alpha_{k}r_{k}r_{k}^{\top}\right)=\frac{\lambda_{\min}(\mathbf{G})}{S_{h}}\geq\frac{(1-\eta)\mu_{G}}{(1+t)\mathbb{E}[S_{h}]}\geq\frac{1-\eta}{1+t}\cdot\frac{a_{0}^{\prime}}{2a_{0}}\,\kappa_{0}=:\tilde{\kappa}_{0}.

Combining with (
D.6
) gives

\mu(U)\ \geq\ \lambda_{0}\,\tilde{\kappa}_{0}\qquad\text{on }\mathcal{E}.

(D.11)

Moreover, by the union bound and (
D.9
)–(
D.10
),

{\mathbb{P}}(\mathcal{E}^{c})\leq\ C_{0}{\sf exp}\!\left(-c_{0}\,\frac{Kh}{\gamma}\right).

(D.12)

6. Conclusion.

Let
A:=\{\lambda_{\min}(\mathbf{S}^{(K)})\leq(1-\varepsilon)\lambda_{0}\tilde{\kappa}_{0}\}
. By the tower property,

{\mathbb{P}}(A)=\mathbb{E}\big[{\mathbb{P}}(A\mid U_{1:K})\big]\leq\mathbb{E}\big[{\mathbb{P}}(A\mid U_{1:K})\mathbf{1}(\mathcal{E})\big]+{\mathbb{P}}(\mathcal{E}^{c}).

On
\mathcal{E}
,
\mu(U)\geq\lambda_{0}\tilde{\kappa}_{0}
by (
D.11
), hence by
(
D.5
),

{\mathbb{P}}(A\mid U_{1:K})\mathbf{1}(\mathcal{E})\leq d_{0}{\sf exp}\!\left(-\frac{\varepsilon^{2}\,\mu(U)}{2R_{0}}\right)\mathbf{1}(\mathcal{E})\leq d_{0}{\sf exp}\!\left(-\frac{\varepsilon^{2}\,\lambda_{0}\tilde{\kappa}_{0}}{2R_{0}}\right)\mathbf{1}(\mathcal{E}).

Recalling
R_{0}=R^{2}b_{0}/K
from (
D.4
), the exponent equals
-c_{3}K

for some
c_{3}>0
.
Therefore,

{\mathbb{P}}(A)\leq d_{0}e^{-c_{3}K}+{\mathbb{P}}(\mathcal{E}^{c})\leq d_{0}e^{-c_{3}K}+C_{0}{\sf exp}\!\left(-c_{0}\,\frac{Kh}{\gamma}\right)\leq{\sf exp}\!\left(-C^{\prime}\,\frac{Kh}{\gamma}\right),

and we have shown that there exist constants
C,C^{\prime}>0
such that

{\mathbb{P}}\!\left\{\lambda_{\min}\!\big(\mathbf{S}^{(K)}\big)\leq C\right\}\ \leq\ {\sf exp}\!\left(-C^{\prime}\,\frac{Kh}{\gamma}\right).

Let the RHS exponent
\frac{Kh}{\gamma}\geq\tfrac{M_{0}}{C^{\prime}}\log(K)
for some constant
M_{0}>1
then it follows that, for some constant
k_{0}
,

\sum_{K=k_{0}}^{\infty}{\mathbb{P}}\!\left\{\lambda_{\min}\!\big(\mathbf{S}^{(K)}\big)\leq C\right\}\ \leq\ \sum_{K=k_{0}}^{\infty}{\sf exp}\!\left(-C^{\prime}\,\frac{Kh}{\gamma}\right)\leq\sum_{K=k_{0}}^{\infty}{\sf exp}\!\left(-M_{0}\log(K)\right)=\sum_{K=k_{0}}^{\infty}K^{-M_{0}}<\infty

Using Borel–Cantelli lemma proves the almost sure result

\lambda_{\min}\!\big(\mathbf{Z}^{\top}\mathbf{W}\mathbf{Z}\big)\geq C

which further implies

\big\|\mathbf{A}_{l}(\mathbf{Z}^{\top}\mathbf{W}\mathbf{Z})^{-1}\mathbf{A}_{l}^{\top}\big\|_{2}\leq\tilde{C}.

4

. The MSE
\pm

, computed across different random seeds, is reported in Table

. The cross-entropy loss
\pm
standard deviation for the analysis in Section
5.2
, also computed across different random seeds, is reported in Table
2
.

We observe that the transfer learning (TL) estimator exhibits adaptivity, effectively selecting the better-performing estimator between the two baselines across different settings.
